E^{\varepsilon}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\frac{|({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*})\cdot\omega|}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf v}^{\prime}-{\bf v}|}{\varepsilon}\Big)\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}-{\bf v}|}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Delta\psi({\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime},{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}\omega.

Let
{\bf z}={\bf v-v}_{*}
. Making change of variable (
2.1
) with
{\bf n}={\bf z}/|{\bf z}|
and using (
2.4
), (
2.2
), and (
2.5
)
we have

(2.26)

L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\Delta\psi({\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime},{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}\sigma,

(2.27)

E^{\varepsilon}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big){\rm d}\theta\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\Delta\psi({\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime},{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}\sigma.

From (
2.18
) we have

(2.28)

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\Delta\psi({\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime},{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})

\displaystyle=-2\pi\big(\nabla\psi({\bf v})-\nabla\psi({\bf v}_{*})\big)\cdot{\bf z}\cos^{2}(\theta)+|{\bf z}|^{2}\cos^{2}(\theta)\sin^{2}(\theta)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\sigma^{\tau}\frac{D^{2}\psi({\bf v})+D^{2}\psi({\bf v}_{*})}{2}\sigma{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle+|{\bf z}|^{2}\cos^{2}(\theta)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)E_{\psi}(t,\theta,\sigma,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}t{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=-2\pi\big(\nabla\psi({\bf v})-\nabla\psi({\bf v}_{*})\big)\cdot{\bf z}\cos^{2}(\theta)+\pi|{\bf z}|^{2}\cos^{2}(\theta)\sin^{2}(\theta)\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\sigma^{\tau}H_{\psi}\sigma{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle+|{\bf z}|^{2}\cos^{2}(\theta)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)E_{\psi}(t,\theta,\sigma,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}t{\rm d}\sigma

where
H_{\psi}=\frac{1}{2}(D^{2}\psi({\bf v}))+D^{2}\psi({\bf v}_{*}))
and
E_{\psi}(t,\theta,\sigma,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})
satisfies

\displaystyle\big|E_{\psi}(t,\theta,\sigma,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\big|\leq 6\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty}\cos(\theta)+2\Lambda_{\psi}\big(|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\big)\sin^{2}(\theta)

\displaystyle{\rm with}\quad\Lambda_{\psi}(\delta)=\sup_{{\bf x,y}\in{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}},|{\bf x-y}|\leq\delta}|D^{2}\psi({\bf x})-D^{2}\psi({\bf y})|,\quad\delta\geq 0.

These imply that ( with
E_{\psi}=E_{\psi}(t,\theta,\sigma,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})
)

\displaystyle L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}\bigg\{-2\pi\big(\nabla\psi({\bf v})-\nabla\psi({\bf v}_{*})\big)\cdot{\bf z}\cos^{2}(\theta)

\displaystyle+\pi|{\bf z}|^{2}\cos^{2}(\theta)\sin^{2}(\theta)\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\sigma^{\tau}H_{\psi}\sigma{\rm d}\sigma+|{\bf z}|^{2}\cos^{2}(\theta)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)E_{\psi}{\rm d}t{\rm d}\sigma\bigg\}{\rm d}\theta

Using Lemma
6.13
, (
1.11
), and Lemma
2.4
we have

(2.29)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\sigma^{\tau}H_{\psi}\sigma{\rm d}\sigma={\rm Tr}(H_{\psi})-{\bf n}^{\tau}H_{\psi}{\bf n},

(2.30)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta=\frac{1}{2\pi|{\bf z}|^{3}},

(2.31)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3+\alpha}(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\theta=0,\qquad\forall\,\alpha>0,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\Lambda_{\psi}\big(|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\big)\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\theta=0.

From these and writing

\cos^{2}(\theta)\sin^{2}(\theta)=\cos^{2}(\theta)-\cos^{4}(\theta)
we derive that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=-2\frac{(\nabla\psi({\bf v})-\nabla\psi({\bf v}_{*}))\cdot{\bf z}}{|{\bf z}|^{3}}+\frac{1}{|{\bf z}|}\big({\rm Tr}(H_{\psi})-{\bf n}^{\tau}H_{\psi}{\bf n}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}\Big({\rm Tr}(H_{\psi})-{\bf n}^{\tau}H_{\psi}{\bf n}-2\frac{(\nabla\psi({\bf v})-\nabla\psi({\bf v}_{*}))\cdot{\bf n}}{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}\Big)=L[\psi]({\bf v,v}_{*}).

Next we prove that

\lim\limits_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}E^{\varepsilon}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=0
so that (
2.23
)
holds true. In fact, by Lemma
2.3
, we first have

|E^{\varepsilon}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|\leq 16\pi\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty}|{\bf z}|^{2}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin^{3}(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|{\rm d}\theta.

Then writing
\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin^{3}(\theta)=\cos^{5/2}(\theta)\sqrt{\sin(\theta)}\cdot\sin^{5/2}(\theta)\sqrt{\cos(\theta)}
and using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
2.31
) we obtain

\displaystyle|E^{\varepsilon}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|\ \leq 16\pi\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty}|{\bf z}|^{2}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{5}(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|^{2}{\rm d}\theta\to 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\varepsilon\to 0^{+}.

Finally to prove (
2.24
), we use

2|ab|\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
, (
2.5
), the second inequality in Lemma
2.3
,
and (
1.11
) to obtain that for all
{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}\in{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}
with
{\bf z}={\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}\neq{\bf 0}
,

\displaystyle|L^{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|\leq|L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|+|\lambda||E^{\varepsilon}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|

\displaystyle\leq 8\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta\bigg|\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\Delta\psi({\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime},{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}\sigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 64\pi\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty}|{\bf z}|^{2}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin^{3}(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta\leq 16\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty}\frac{1}{|{\bf z}|}.

This also gives

|L[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|=\lim\limits_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}|L^{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|\leq 16\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty}\frac{1}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|}.

∎

2.3.
A general lemma on weak convergence.

The following lemma are often used
in dealing with weak convergence and it can be easily proved by induction on the number
k
of factors.

Lemma 2.6
.

(1)
Let
X\subset{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d}
be a Borel set,
\mu\geq 0
a regular Borel
measure on
X
, and let

f^{n},f\in L^{1}(X,{\rm d}\mu)
satisfy
f^{n}\rightharpoonup f\,(n\to\infty)
weakly in

L^{1}(X,{\rm d}\mu)
. Let

\psi_{n},\psi
be Borel measurable functions on
X
satisfying

\sup_{n\geq 1,{\bf x}\in X}|\psi_{n}({\bf x})|<\infty,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\psi_{n}({\bf x})=\psi({\bf x}),\quad\mu-{\rm a.e.}\quad{\bf x}\in X.

Then

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{X}\psi_{n}({\bf x})f^{n}({\bf x})\mu({\bf x})=\int_{X}\psi({\bf x})f({\bf x}){\rm d}\mu({\bf x}).

(2) Let
k,d_{j}\in{{\mathbb{N}}}
,
and let
X_{j}\subset{{\mathbb{R}}}^{d_{j}}
be a Borel set,
\mu_{j}\geq 0
a regular Borel
measure on
X_{j}
, and let

f_{j}^{n},f_{j}\in L^{1}(X_{j},{\rm d}\mu_{j})
satisfy
f^{n}_{j}\rightharpoonup f_{j}\,(n\to\infty)
weakly in

L^{1}(X_{j},{\rm d}\mu_{j}),j=1,2,...,k
. Let

X=X_{1}\times X_{2}\times\cdots\times X_{k}
,
\mu=\mu_{1}\otimes\mu_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mu_{k}
(product measure), and let

\Psi_{n},\Psi
be Borel measurable functions on
X
satisfying

\sup_{n\geq 1,{\bf x}\in X}|\Psi_{n}({\bf x})|<\infty,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\Psi_{n}({\bf x})=\Psi({\bf x}),\quad\mu-{\rm a.e.}\quad{\bf x}=({\bf x}_{1},{\bf x}_{2},...,{\bf x}_{k})\in X.

Then

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{X}\Psi_{n}({\bf x})f_{1}^{n}({\bf x}_{1})f_{2}^{n}({\bf x}_{2})\cdots f_{k}^{n}({\bf x}_{k}){\rm d}\mu({\bf x})=\int_{X}\Psi({\bf x})f_{1}({\bf x}_{1})f_{2}({\bf x}_{2})\cdots f_{k}({\bf x}_{k}){\rm d}\mu({\bf x}).

3.
Proof of Theorem
1.12

In this section we prove Theorem
1.12
(from Eq.(MB) to Eq.(FPL)). In the proof,
the entropy inequality and entropy dissipation
D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t))
of Eq.(MB) will be sufficiently used to prove that the weak limit
f(t,{\bf v})
of

f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})
satisfies that the function
(t,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\mapsto\sqrt{f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})/|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}
has the weak projection gradient in
{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}\neq{\bf 0}
.

Proof of Theorem
1.12
(From Eq.(MB) to Eq.(FPL)).

The proof consists of several steps. We first recall that
f^{\varepsilon}\in L^{\infty}([0,\infty),L^{1}_{2}\cap L\log L({{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}))
with the initial data
f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\in L^{1}_{2}\cap L\log L({{\mathbb{R}}}^{3})

are weak solutions of Eq.(MB) given in the theorem.

In the following, we assume without loss of generality that
0<\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1
, and we denote by
C_{0}
the positive and finite constants that depend only on
the initial bounds
\sup\limits_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\|f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}
and
\sup\limits_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\log f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}
, and

C_{0}
may have different value in different lines.
As is well-known, we first have

(3.1)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0},t\geq 0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})(1+|{\bf v}|^{2}+|\log f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})|){\rm d}{\bf v}\leq C_{0},

(3.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\int_{0}^{\infty}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t)){\rm d}t\leq C_{0}.

Given any sequence
{\cal E}=\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
satisfying
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0\,(n\to\infty)
.

Step 1.
We prove that
there exists a subsequence of
{\cal E}
, still
denote it by
{\cal E}
, and a function
f\in L^{\infty}([0,\infty),L^{1}_{2}\cap L\log L({{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}))
, such that

(3.3)

f^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)\rightharpoonup f(t,\cdot)\quad(\,{\rm as}\,\,\,{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0\,)\quad{\rm weakly\,\,in}\,\,\,L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{3})\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0

and
f
conserves the mass, momentum, and energy.

We first prove that for any
t\in[0,\infty)
, any
\psi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{3})
, and any
measurable function
\zeta(r)
on

{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
satisfying

0\leq\zeta(r)\leq 1
on
r\in[0,\infty)
,

(3.4)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}\zeta(|{\bf v-v}_{*}|)B_{0}^{\varepsilon}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)\big|{f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}-{f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}\big|\big|\psi+\psi_{*}-\psi^{\prime}-\psi_{*}^{\prime}\big|{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\|D^{2}\psi\|_{*}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\zeta(|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|)f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{*}(1+|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})^{1/2}|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}\bigg)^{1/2}\sqrt{D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t))}.

In fact, using the inequality

|a-b|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{a+b}\sqrt{\Gamma(a,b)}\,(a,b\geq 0)
where
\Gamma(a,b)
is given in (
1.18
), we have

\big|{f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}-{f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}\big|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{{f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}+f^{\varepsilon}f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}\sqrt{\Gamma\big({f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime},\,f^{\varepsilon}f_{*}^{\varepsilon}\big)}.

By using the first inequality in Lemma
2.3
and
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we deduce that

\displaystyle{\rm the\,\,l.h.s.\,\,of\,\,(\ref{3.7})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\|D^{2}\psi\|_{*}}{2}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\zeta(|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|)f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{*}(1+|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})^{1/2}|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{4}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)\sin^{2}(\theta)\cos^{2}(\theta){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}\bigg)^{1/2}\sqrt{D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t))}

where
\theta=\arccos({\bf n}\cdot\omega)
,
{\bf n}=({\bf v-v}_{*})/|{\bf v-v}_{*}|
.
Then using (
1.23
) gives (
3.4
).

Since
0\leq\zeta(\cdot)\leq 1
,

(1+|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})^{1/2}|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\leq(1+|{\bf v}|^{2})(1+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})
, and

f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})
conserves the mass and energy, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\zeta(|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|)f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{*}(1+|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})^{1/2}|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}\leq\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{1}_{2}}^{2}=\|f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}^{2}.

In particular for the case
\zeta(r)\equiv 1

we obtain from (
3.4
) that

(3.5)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)\big|{f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}-{f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}\big|\big|\psi+\psi_{*}-\psi^{\prime}-\psi_{*}^{\prime}\big|{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\|D^{2}\psi\|_{*}\|f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}\sqrt{D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t))}

and so for all
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0

(3.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t_{1},{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t_{2},{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\|D^{2}\psi\|_{*}\sup_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}\int_{t_{1}\wedge t_{2}}^{t_{1}\vee t_{2}}\sqrt{D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t))}{\rm d}t\leq C_{0}\|D^{2}\psi\|_{*}\sqrt{|t_{2}-t_{1}|}.

From this uniform estimate and standard smooth
approximation, it is easily proved that for any
\psi\in L^{\infty}({{\mathbb{R}}}^{3})
,

(3.7)

\sup_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0},|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq\delta}\bigg|\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t_{1},{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}-\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t_{2},{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}\bigg|\to 0\quad(\delta\to 0).

Now from (
3.1
), (
3.7
) and Dunford-Petties criterion of

L^{1}
-weakly relative compactness, we conclude that there exist a subsequence of
{\cal E}=\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, still
denote it by
{\cal E}
, and a function

f\in L^{\infty}([0,\infty),L^{1}_{2}\cap L\log L({{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}))
, such that (
3.3
)
hold true.

It is easily seen that the weak liming function
f
conserves that mass and momentum.
To prove the conservation of energy,
we consider a smooth cutoff approximation: let
\zeta\in C^{\infty}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
satisfies

0\leq\zeta\leq 1
on
{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
and

\zeta(r)=1
for
r\in[0,1]
,
\zeta(r)=0
for
r\geq 2
, and let

\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})=\varphi_{\delta}(|{\bf v}|^{2}/2),\qquad\varphi_{\delta}(r)=r\zeta(\delta r),\qquad\delta>0.

Then
\psi_{\delta}\in C^{2}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}),0\leq\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})\leq|{\bf v}|^{2}/2
, and
\lim\limits_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})=|{\bf v}|^{2}/2
.
Compute

\displaystyle|\varphi_{\delta}^{\prime\prime}(r)|r^{3/4}=\big|2\zeta^{\prime}(\delta r)(\delta r)^{3/4}+(\delta r)^{7/4}\zeta^{\prime\prime}(\delta r)\big|\delta^{1/4}\leq C\delta^{1/4}

with
C=\sup\limits_{r\geq 0}\big|2\zeta^{\prime}(r)r^{3/4}+r^{7/4}\zeta^{\prime\prime}(r)\big|.
Using (
2.19
) gives
\|D^{2}\psi_{\delta}\|_{*}\leq 8\sup\limits_{r\geq 0}|\varphi_{\delta}^{\prime\prime}(r)|r^{3/4}\leq 8C\delta^{1/4}
and so
by weak convergence and (
3.6
) we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})f_{0}({\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}\bigg|=\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}_{0}({\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|D^{2}\psi_{\delta}\|_{*}\sqrt{t}\leq C_{0}\delta^{1/4}\sqrt{t}\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0,\,\forall\,\delta>0.

Letting
\delta\to 0^{+}
we obtain using dominated convergence theorem that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{|{\bf v}|^{2}}{2}f(t,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\frac{|{\bf v}|^{2}}{2}f_{0}({\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi_{\delta}({\bf v})f_{0}({\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}\bigg)=0

for all
t\in[0,\infty)
. So
f
also conserves energy.

Step 2.

In what follows, the function
f(t,{\bf v})
is always the
L^{1}
-weak
limit of
f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})\,({\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0)
obtained in Step 1.
Let

\displaystyle F(t,{\bf v,v}_{*})=f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})\frac{1}{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|},

\displaystyle F_{\delta}(t,{\bf v,v}_{*})=f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})\frac{1}{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}+\delta e^{-(|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})},

\displaystyle F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v,v}_{*})=f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v}_{*})\frac{1}{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}+\delta e^{-(|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})},\quad\delta>0.

By change of variables
{\bf v}=\frac{{\bf w}+{\bf z}}{2}
and
{\bf v}_{*}=\frac{{\bf w}-{\bf z}}{2}
, we also denote

(3.8)

\displaystyle\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=F\big(t,\frac{{\bf w}+{\bf z}}{2},\frac{{\bf w}-{\bf z}}{2}\big),\quad\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}\big(t,\frac{{\bf w}+{\bf z}}{2},\frac{{\bf w}-{\bf z}}{2}\big).

Due to the
L^{1}
- weak convergence proved in Step 1, it is easily seen that
for any
\varphi=\varphi({\bf w},{\bf z})\in L^{\infty}({{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}})
and any
\psi=\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\in L^{\infty}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}})
,

(3.9)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\varphi\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}=\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\varphi\bar{F}_{\delta}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,\infty),

(3.10)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\psi\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\psi\bar{F}_{\delta}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

For any
0<T<\infty
, using the above convergence to the function

1_{[0,T]}(t)e^{t}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})
we have

(3.11)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\psi\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\psi\bar{F}_{\delta}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

for all
\psi\in L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}).

Our goal of this step is to prove the following weak
convergence: for any
\delta>0
and any bounded Borel measurable function

\psi(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)
on
[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)\times{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
we have

(3.12)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}r\bigg)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

where the function
\varrho(r)
is given in (
2.12
).

Before going on, we first note that if (
3.12
) holds true, then
for any
0<T<\infty
and
any bounded Borel measurable function

\psi(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)
on
[0,T]\times[0,\infty)\times{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
,
using (
3.12
) to the bounded function

1_{0,T]}(t)e^{t}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
gives the convergence

(3.13)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}r\bigg)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

To prove (
3.12
) and for notational convergence, we will use
a Borel measure space

(X,{\mathscr{B}},\mu)
where

(3.14)

X=[0,\infty)\times[0,\infty)\times{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}},

\mu
is defined for every Borel set
E\subset X
by

(3.15)

\mu(E):=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\bigg(\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}1_{E}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma\bigg){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t

with
{\bf n}={\bf z}/|{\bf z}|
, and we denote
{\rm d}\mu={\rm d}\mu(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
.
Note: one may use the complete measure space
(X,{\mathscr{M}},\bar{\mu})
which is the completion of

(X,{\mathscr{B}},\mu)
. Since, as is well-known, every
{\mathscr{M}}
-measurable function can be
modified on a Borel set of
\mu
-measure zero such that after the modification the function is
Borel measurable, we can assume that all functions appeared are Borel measurable. In other words,
when concerning measurability and integrability, we do not distinguish between
a function and its modification on a set of measure zero.
By changing variable
\omega=\cos(\theta){\bf n}+\sin(\theta)\sigma
, we see that for any nonnegative or bounded Borel measurable function
\varphi
on

{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
, it holds

(3.16)

\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\varphi(\sigma){\rm d}\sigma=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}\varphi\Big(\frac{\omega-({\bf n}\cdot\omega){\bf n}}{\sqrt{1-({\bf n}\cdot\omega)^{2}}}\Big){\rm d}\omega\qquad\forall\,{\bf n}\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}

where for the cases
\omega=\pm{\bf n}
we define for instance
\frac{\omega-({\bf n}\cdot\omega){\bf n}}{\sqrt{1-({\bf n}\cdot\omega)^{2}}}=(1,0,0)^{\tau}
. The equality (
3.16
) shows that
\mu
is really a Borel measure on
the product set
X
.
Also from
0\leq\varrho\in L^{1}([0,\infty))
one sees that
\mu(K)<\infty
for any compact set

K\subset X
and thus by measure theory (see e.g. W. Rudin
[
26
]
, Chapter 2),

\mu
is a regular Borel measure.
This regularity allows us to use Lusin’s theorem on continuous approximation and the criterion of
L^{1}
-weak compactness.

It is easy to see that for any
\mu
-integrable or nonnegative Borel measurable functions
F(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma),\\
G(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})
on
X
,

\displaystyle\int_{X}F(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\mu=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}F(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}r{\rm d}t,

\displaystyle\int_{X}G(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu=2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}G(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

By using Lemma
2.2
, we derive that for any Borel measurable
function
\varphi\geq 0
on
X\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}
,

(3.17)

\int_{X}\varphi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma,{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu=\int_{X}\varphi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}^{*},\sigma_{\varepsilon r},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu.

And for any Borel set
E\subset X
, the set

E^{\varepsilon}:=\{(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\in X\,\,|\,\,(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r},\sigma_{\varepsilon r})\in E\},\quad\varepsilon>0

is also a Borel set in
X
and

(3.18)

\mu(E^{\varepsilon})=\mu(E)\qquad\forall\,\varepsilon>0.

In fact, the definition of

{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r},\sigma_{\varepsilon r}
implies that
E^{\varepsilon}
is a Borel set, and the equality (
3.18
)
follows from

1_{E}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r},\sigma_{\varepsilon r})=1_{E^{\varepsilon}}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)

and the formula (
3.17
).

Now for any bounded Borel measurable function
\psi(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)

on
X
,
applying
L^{1}
-weak convergence (
3.10
) to
bounded Borel measurable function
(t,{\bf w,z})\mapsto\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}r
,
we have

(3.19)

\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{X}\psi(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu=\int_{X}\psi(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu.

This weak convergence and the criterion of
L^{1}
-weak compactness
(Dunford-Pettis theorem) imply that
the set
\{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}\}_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}
is relatively weakly compact in

L^{1}(X,{\rm d}\mu)
and thus we have
the following uniform integrability (with
\delta>0
fixed):

(3.20)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},\,\mu(E)\leq\eta}\int_{E}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\to 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\eta\to 0^{+}

where
E\subset X
are Borel sets.
Note that from (
3.17
)
and
1_{E}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r},\sigma_{\varepsilon r})=1_{E^{\varepsilon}}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
we have

(3.21)

\int_{E}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu=\int_{E^{\varepsilon}}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\qquad\forall\,{\rm Borel\,\,set\,\,}\,E\subset X.

Since
\mu(E^{\varepsilon})=\mu(E)
, it follows that

(3.22)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},\,\mu(E)\leq\eta}\int_{E}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu\leq\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},\,\mu(E)\leq\eta}\int_{E}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\to 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\eta\to 0^{+}.

Let
\psi(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)
be a bounded Borel measurable function
on
X
. For any
1<R<\infty
, let

\psi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)=\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)1_{\{|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2}\leq R^{2}\}}.

Since
\mu
is a regular measure and

\mu(\{(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\in X\,|\,|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2}\leq R^{2}\})<\infty
,
it follows from Lusin’s theorem that there is
function
\varphi_{R}\in C_{c}(X)
such that

\displaystyle\mu(E_{R})<\frac{1}{R},\quad\|\varphi_{R}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\psi\|_{\infty}.

where

E_{R}=\big\{(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\in X\,\,|\,\,\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\neq\psi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big\}.

Then we have

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{X}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu-\int_{X}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{X}\big|\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)-\psi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big|\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{X}\big|\psi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)-\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big|\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{X}\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu-\int_{X}\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu\bigg|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{X}\big|\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)-\psi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)|\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{X}\big|\psi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)-\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big|\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon,R}+J_{\varepsilon,R}+|\Delta_{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{R})|+J_{R}+I_{R}.

Estimate of
I_{\varepsilon,R}
:
Using Lemma
2.2
again we have

\displaystyle I_{\varepsilon,R}\leq 2\pi\|\psi\|_{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2}>R^{2}\}}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

From (
3.10
) we know that
\{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}\}_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}
is
weakly compact in
L^{1}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},e^{-t}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t)
, which
implies that

\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}I_{\varepsilon,R}\leq 2\pi\|\psi\|_{\infty}\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2}>R^{2}\}}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\to 0

as
R\to\infty.

Estimate of
I_{R}
:  From the integrability
\bar{F}_{\delta}\in L^{1}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},e^{-t}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t)
,
one has

\displaystyle I_{R}\leq 2\pi\|\psi\|_{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2}>R^{2}\}}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\to 0\quad{\rm as}\quad R\to\infty.

Estimate of
J_{\varepsilon,R}
:
Since

\mu(E_{R})<\frac{1}{R}
,
it follows from (
3.22
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}J_{\varepsilon,R}\leq 2\|\psi\|_{\infty}\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},\,\mu(E)<1/R}\int_{E}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}\mu\to 0\quad{\rm as}\,\,\,R\to\infty.

Estimate of
J_{R}
:  From
\bar{F}_{\delta}\in L^{1}(X,{\rm d}\mu)
,
we have

\displaystyle J_{R}\leq 2\|\psi\|_{\infty}\int_{E_{R}}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\to 0\quad{\rm as}\quad R\to\infty.

Estimate of
\Delta_{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{R})
:  Using (
2.14
) we have

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\varepsilon}(\varphi_{R})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{X}\big|\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r},\sigma_{\varepsilon r})-\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big|\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{X}\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu-\int_{X}\varphi_{R}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\bigg|

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon,R}+L_{\varepsilon,R}.

By the
L^{1}
-weak convergence obtained above we have

L_{\varepsilon,R}\to 0
as
{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0.

To estimate
K_{\varepsilon,R}
, we make a decomposition as follows:

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon,R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{X}1_{\{|{\bf z}|<1/R\}}\{\cdots\}{\rm d}\mu+\int_{X}\big(1_{\{0<r<1/R\}}+1_{\{r>R\}}\big)1_{\{|{\bf z}|\geq 1/R\}}\{\cdots\}{\rm d}\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\int_{X}1_{\{1/R\leq r\leq R\}}1_{\{|{\bf z}|\geq 1/R\}}\{\cdots\}{\rm d}\mu:=K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(1)}+K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(2)}+K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(3)}.

We have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(1)}\leq 4\pi\|\psi\|_{\infty}\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{|{\bf z}|<1/R}\bigg(\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf w}\bigg){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t\to 0\quad{\rm as}\,\,\,R\to\infty

since

\{\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf w}\}_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}
is
weakly compact in
L^{1}([0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}},e^{-t}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t)
.
Next we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(2)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\|\psi\|_{\infty}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1/R}\varrho(r){\rm d}r+\int_{R}^{\infty}\varrho(r){\rm d}r\bigg)2\pi\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\psi}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1/R}\varrho(r){\rm d}r+\int_{R}^{\infty}\varrho(r){\rm d}r\bigg)\to 0\quad{\rm as}\,\,\,R\to\infty.

For the term
K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(3)}
,
since
\varphi_{R}\in C_{c}(X)
is uniformally continuous on
X
and
since

|{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r}-{\bf z}|+|\sigma_{\varepsilon r}-\sigma|\leq 2\varepsilon r+\frac{2\varepsilon r}{|{\bf z}|}\leq 2\varepsilon R+2\varepsilon R^{2}\leq 4R^{2}\varepsilon

for all
r\in[1/R,R],|{\bf z}|\geq 1/R,\sigma\in{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})
, it follows that

K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(3)}\to 0
as
{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0.

Thus first letting
\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}|\Delta_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|\leq\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}I_{\varepsilon,R}+\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}J_{\varepsilon,R}+\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(1)}+\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}K_{\varepsilon,R}^{(2)}+J_{R}+I_{R}

and then letting
R\to\infty
we conclude

\limsup\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}|\Delta_{\varepsilon}(\psi)|=0.

This proves the weak convergence (
3.12
).

Step 3.

In this step we prove that the square root

\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}}
has the weak projection gradient

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}
in
{\bf z}\in{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\setminus\{{\bf 0}\}
(see Definition
6.4
) and, for any
T\in(0,\infty)
,

(3.23)

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta>0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Big|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\leq\liminf_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})-H(f(T)),\quad

(3.24)

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta>0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Big|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\leq C_{0}.

We emphasize that
C_{0}
depends only on the bounds

\sup\limits_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\|f_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}
and
\sup\limits_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\log f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}}
.

In order to prove (
3.23
),
we choose a subsequence
\widetilde{{\cal E}}=\{\varepsilon_{n_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
of

{\cal E}=\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
such that

\lim_{\widetilde{{\cal E}}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})=\liminf_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0}).

For notational convenience we still denote
\widetilde{{\cal E}}
by
{\cal E}
, i.e. we may assume
that

\lim\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})
exists.
Observe that for any

a,b\geq 0
,
c>0
and
0\leq\eta\leq 1
, we have (the case
a=b=0
is obvious)

\displaystyle\Gamma(a,b)\geq 2\frac{(a-b)^{2}}{a+b}\eta=2\frac{(a\eta-b\eta)^{2}}{a\eta+b\eta}\geq 2\frac{(a\eta+c-(b\eta+c))^{2}}{a\eta+c+b\eta+c}.

So using also the collision invariance

|{\bf v}^{\prime}-{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}|=|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|,|{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}=|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2}
we get

\displaystyle\Gamma\big({f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f^{\varepsilon}}_{*}^{\prime},f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{*}\big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\frac{\big({f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f^{\varepsilon}}_{*}^{\prime}1_{\{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}+c-(f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{*}1_{\{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}+c)\big)^{2}}{{f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f^{\varepsilon}}_{*}^{\prime}1_{\{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}+c+f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{*}1_{\{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}+c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\frac{\big(F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime})-F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\big)^{2}}{F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime})+F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})},

where
c=|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\delta e^{-(|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})}
and we have taken
\eta=1_{\{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}
. Then using the second equality (
2.13
) in Lemma
2.1
yields that

\displaystyle D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}B^{\varepsilon}_{0}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)\Gamma\big({f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f^{\varepsilon}}_{*}^{\prime},{f^{\varepsilon}}{f^{\varepsilon}}_{*}\big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}B^{\varepsilon}_{0}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)2|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\frac{\big(F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime})-F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\big)^{2}}{F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v}^{\prime},{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime})+F^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\frac{\Big(\frac{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})-\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{2\varepsilon r}\Big)^{2}}{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})+\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}r{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}.

Introduce

D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma):=1_{\{\varepsilon\leq r\leq 1/\varepsilon\}}\frac{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})-\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{2\varepsilon r}.

Then from the above inequality we have

(3.25)

\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\frac{\big(D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})+\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}r{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}\leq 2\pi D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)).

In what follows, we will
use the measure
\mu
on

X
defined in Step 2.
Then from (
3.25
), we have

\int_{X}\frac{\big(D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})+\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\mu\leq 2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)){\rm d}t\leq 2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)){\rm d}t\leq C_{0}

which implies that
for any nonnegative Borel measurable function

\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
on

X
,

(3.26)

\displaystyle\int_{X}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)|D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)|{\rm d}\mu\leq\bigg(\int_{X}\frac{\big(D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})+\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\mu\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\int_{X}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)^{2}\Big(\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})+\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\Big){\rm d}\mu\bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{C_{0}}\bigg(\int_{X}\big(\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}^{*},\sigma_{\varepsilon r})^{2}+\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)^{2}\big)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\bigg)^{1/2}.

Here in the last inequality we have used the equality
(
3.17
).
Recalling
\varrho\in L^{1}([0,\infty))
and a fact from measure theory we know that there is a non-decreasing function
l(r)>0
on
[0,\infty)
such that

\lim_{r\to\infty}l(r)=\infty,\quad\int_{0}^{\infty}l(r)\varrho(r){\rm d}r<\infty.

[For instance,
l(r)=(A(r))^{-1/2}
where
A(r)=\int_{r}^{\infty}\varrho(s){\rm d}s+e^{-r}.
The function
(A(r))^{1/2}
is absolutely
continuous and
A^{\prime}(r)=-\varrho(r)-e^{-r}
a.e. in
(0,\infty)

and so
\int_{0}^{\infty}l(r)\varrho(r){\rm d}r\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}-(A(r))^{-1/2}A^{\prime}(r){\rm d}r=\lim\limits_{R\to\infty}-2(A(r))^{1/2}\big|_{r=0}^{r=R}<\infty
.] If we choose
\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)=(1+t+|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2}+l(r))^{1/2}
, then
using the identity
|{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}^{*}|\equiv|{\bf z}|
and
(1+t+l(r)+|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2})\leq(1+t)(1+l(r))(1+|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2})

we have from (
3.26
) that

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\int_{X}(1+t+l(r)+|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2})^{1/2}|D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)|{\rm d}\mu

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\bigg(\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}(1+|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2})\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}\bigg)^{1/2}<\infty

where the inequality “
<\infty
” is due to
\frac{1}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}\leq 1/\delta
and
\sup\limits_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\|f^{\varepsilon}(t)\|_{L^{1}_{2}}=\sup\limits_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}<\infty
(by conservation of mass
and energy).
Next for any Borel set
E\subset X
, applying
(
3.26
) to the function
\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)=1_{E}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)

gives

\displaystyle\int_{E}|D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)|{\rm d}\mu\leq\sqrt{C_{0}}\bigg(\int_{E^{\varepsilon}}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu+\int_{E}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\bigg)^{1/2}.

Hence from
\mu(E^{\varepsilon})=\mu(E)
and (
3.20
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\sup_{\mu(E)\leq\eta}\int_{E}|D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)|{\rm d}\mu\leq\sqrt{C_{0}}\bigg(2\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\sup_{\mu(E)\leq\eta}\int_{E}\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu\bigg)^{1/2}\to 0\,\,\,\,{\rm as}\,\,\eta\to 0^{+}.

By the criterion of
L^{1}
-relatively weak compactness
(Dunford-Pettis theorem), with
\delta
fixed,
there exist a subsequence
{\cal E}_{\delta}:=\{\varepsilon_{n_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}\subset{\cal E}
(which may depend on
\delta
) and
a function

D_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\in L^{1}(X,{\rm d}\mu)
such
that for any bounded Borel measurable
function
\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
on
X
,

(3.27)

\lim_{{\cal E}_{\delta}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{X}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\mu=\int_{X}\psi(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)D_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\mu.

Let us define

D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma):=\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}r,\quad D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma):=\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)D_{\delta}(t,r,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}r.

Then for any bounded Borel measurable function
\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
and for any
0<T<\infty
, applying (
3.27
)
with the function
1_{[0,T]}(t)e^{t}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
, we obtain that

(3.28)

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}_{\delta}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

Now we are going to prove that
D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
satisfies the
conditions in Lemma
6.16
with
Y=[0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}
and

{\bf y}=(t,{\bf w})
.
First by definition of
D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)
we have

D_{\delta}\in L^{1}([0,T]\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}},{\rm d}\nu)
for all
0<T<\infty
,
where the measure
\nu
is given in Lemma
6.16
.
Take any
\psi\in{\cal T}_{c}([0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}})
(recall (
6.32
) for
Y=[0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}
and

{\bf y}=(t,{\bf w})
). By definition, there exists
0<T<\infty
such that
\psi
is supported in
[0,T]\times{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
. Then, thanks to the cutoff
1_{\{\varepsilon\leq r\leq 1/\varepsilon\}}
,
Lemma
2.2
yields that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)1_{\{\varepsilon\leq r\leq 1/\varepsilon\}}\frac{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})-\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{2\varepsilon r}{\rm d}r\bigg){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)1_{\{\varepsilon\leq r\leq 1/\varepsilon\}}\frac{\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r},\sigma_{\varepsilon r})-\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)}{2\varepsilon r}{\rm d}r\bigg)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{X}1_{[0,T]}(t)e^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu,

where

{\mathscr{D}}_{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma):=1_{\{\varepsilon\leq r\leq 1/\varepsilon\}}\frac{\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r},\sigma_{\varepsilon r})-\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)}{2\varepsilon r}.

For any

(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)\in[0,T]\times(0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times\{{\bf 0}\})\times{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
,
let
\varepsilon>0
be so small that we can use local mean-value theorem of differentation to
get some
\widetilde{{\bf z}}_{\varepsilon r}={\bf z}+\theta({\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon}-{\bf z}),\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varepsilon r}=\sigma+\theta(\sigma_{\varepsilon r}-\sigma)
with
0<\theta<1
(depending on
t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma,\varepsilon,r
) such that (with (
2.6
)-(
2.10
))

\displaystyle{\mathscr{D}}_{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1_{\{\varepsilon\leq r\leq 1/\varepsilon\}}\bigg(\nabla_{{\bf z}}\psi(t,{\bf w},\widetilde{{\bf z}}_{\varepsilon r},\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varepsilon r})\cdot\frac{{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r}-{\bf z}}{2\varepsilon r}+\nabla_{\sigma}\psi(t,{\bf w},\widetilde{{\bf z}}_{\varepsilon r},\widetilde{\sigma}_{\varepsilon r})\cdot\frac{\sigma_{\varepsilon r}-\sigma}{2\varepsilon r}\bigg)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle-\nabla_{{\bf z}}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\cdot\sigma+\nabla_{\sigma}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\cdot\frac{{\bf n}}{|{\bf z}|}\quad{\rm as}\,\,\,\varepsilon\to 0^{+}.

Also using Lemma
6.14
and (
2.10
) we have

\displaystyle|{\mathscr{D}}_{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)|\leq\|\nabla_{{\bf z}}\psi\|_{\infty}\frac{|{\bf z}^{*}_{\varepsilon r}-{\bf z}|}{2\varepsilon r}+4\sqrt{2}\|\nabla_{{\sigma}}\psi(t,{\bf w,z},\cdot)\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}\frac{|\sigma_{\varepsilon r}-\sigma|}{2\varepsilon r}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{{\bf z}}\psi\|_{\infty}+4\sqrt{2}\Big\|\frac{1}{|{\bf z}|}\nabla_{{\sigma}}\psi(t,{\bf w,z},\cdot)\Big\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}.

Thus the function
1_{[0,T]}(t)e^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)1_{\{{\bf z}\neq{\bf 0},r>0\}}
is bounded on
X
.
Recalling the weak convergence
(
3.19
) , i.e.

\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}\rightharpoonup\bar{F}_{\delta}\,({\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0)
weakly in

L^{1}(X,{\rm d}\mu)
,
we can use part (1) of Lemma
2.6
to conclude the convergence:

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{X}1_{[0,T]}(t)e^{t}{\mathscr{D}}_{\psi}^{\varepsilon}(t,r,{\bf w,z},\sigma)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu

\displaystyle=\int_{X}1_{[0,T]}(t)e^{t}\Big(-\nabla_{{\bf z}}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\cdot\sigma+\nabla_{\sigma}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\cdot\frac{{\bf n}}{|{\bf z}|}\Big)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\mu.

Collecting the above results we derive that

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}_{\delta}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\Big(-\nabla_{{\bf z}}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\cdot\sigma+\nabla_{\sigma}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\cdot\frac{{\bf n}}{|{\bf z}|}\Big)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

From this together (
3.28
) we finally obtain that for any
\psi\in{\cal T}_{c}([0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}})

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\Big(\nabla_{{\bf z}}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\cdot\sigma-\nabla_{\sigma}\psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\cdot\frac{{\bf n}}{|{\bf z}|}\Big)\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

By Lemma
6.16
with
Y=[0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}
and

{\bf y}=(t,{\bf w})

we conclude that the weak projection gradient

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{\bf z}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})
exists and (up to a set of measure zero)

(3.29)

\displaystyle\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{\bf z}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\sigma{\rm d}\sigma,

(3.30)

\displaystyle D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)=(\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{\bf z}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}))\cdot\sigma.

And by Lemma
6.10
, the weak projection gradient

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{\bf z}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}
also exists and

(3.31)

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{\bf z}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}=\frac{\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{\bf z}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{2\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}}.

Next in order to prove the entropy inequality (
3.23
), we consider
convexity argument. The function

(x,y,z)\mapsto\frac{x^{2}}{y+z}
is
convex in
(x,y,z)\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{2}
so that we have

(3.32)

\frac{x^{2}}{y+z}\geq\frac{x_{0}^{2}}{y_{0}+z_{0}}+\frac{2x_{0}}{y_{0}+z_{0}}(x-x_{0})-\frac{x_{0}^{2}}{(y_{0}+z_{0})^{2}}(y-y_{0})-\frac{x_{0}^{2}}{(y_{0}+z_{0})^{2}}(z-z_{0})

for all
(x,y,z),(x_{0},y_{0},z_{0})\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}\times{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{2}.

For any
0<T<\infty,0<R<\infty
, let

S_{R}=\big\{(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\in[0,T]\times{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\,\,\,|\,\,\,|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2}\leq R^{2},\,|D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)|\leq R\big\}.

Then using (
3.32
) to

x=D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma),y=\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}),z=\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}),x_{0}=D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma),y_{0}=z_{0}=\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})

and then multiplying both sides of (
3.32
) by
1_{S_{R}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)

and then taking integration we have

(3.33)

\displaystyle\quad\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)1_{S_{R}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\frac{\big(D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})+\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}r{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}1_{S_{R}}\frac{\big(D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{2\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}1_{S_{R}}\frac{D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)}{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\big(D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)-D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}1_{S_{R}}\frac{\big(D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{\big(2\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\big)^{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r}){\rm d}r-\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\bigg){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}1_{S_{R}}\frac{\big(D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{\big(2\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\big)^{2}}\big(\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})-\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\big){\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

where we have used
\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r){\rm d}r=1
.
Since

\inf\limits_{t\geq 0,|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2}\leq R^{2}}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})>0,

the functions

1_{S_{R}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\frac{D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)}{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})},1_{S_{R}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\frac{(D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma))^{2}}{(2\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}))^{2}}
are all bounded in

[0,T]\times{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}
. Thus letting
{\cal E}_{\delta}\ni\varepsilon\to 0
we
obtain from the weak convergences (
3.13
), (
3.11
) and (
3.28
) that
(recall notation in (
1.50
))

\displaystyle\liminf_{{\cal E}_{\delta}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho(r)1_{S_{R}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\frac{\big(D^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}_{\varepsilon r})+\bar{F}^{\varepsilon}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}1_{S_{R}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\frac{\big(D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{2\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

This together with (
3.25
) and

\liminf\limits_{{\cal E}_{\delta}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\leq\limsup\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}

gives

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}1_{S_{R}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\frac{\big(D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{2\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\leq 2\pi\limsup_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)){\rm d}t.

Letting
R\to\infty
we conclude from the monotone convergence theorem that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\frac{\big(D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)\big)^{2}}{2\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\leq 2\pi\limsup_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)){\rm d}t\qquad\forall\,T>0.

Since
D_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z},\sigma)=(\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{\bf z}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}))\cdot\sigma
and (using the second equality in (
6.31
))

\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\big(\big(\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\big)\cdot\sigma\big)^{2}{\rm d}\sigma=\pi\big|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\big|^{2},

it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\frac{|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})|^{2}}{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t\ \leq\limsup_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)){\rm d}t\qquad\forall\,T>0.

Then using the equality (
3.31
) we conclude

(3.34)

\sup_{\delta>0}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Big|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\leq\limsup_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)){\rm d}t\quad\forall\,T>0.

On the other hand, from the entropy inequality (
1.21
)
we have

\limsup\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)){\rm d}t\leq\limsup\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})-\liminf\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}(T)).

Thanks to the
L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
-weak convergence
f^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)\rightharpoonup f(t,\cdot)
(as
{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0
for every
t\geq 0
) and that
f\mapsto f\log f
is convex, one has

\liminf\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}(T))\geq H(f(T)).

Since the limit
\lim\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})
already exists, this gives

\limsup_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}D_{0}^{\varepsilon}({f^{\varepsilon}}(t)){\rm d}t\leq\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})-H(f(T)).

This together with (
3.34
) gives (
3.23
).
From (
3.23
) and letting
T\to\infty
we also obtain (
3.24
).

Step 4
. In this step we prove that
the weak projection gradient

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}
exists and satisfies

(3.35)

\int_{0}^{T}{\rm d}t\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Big|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}\leq\liminf_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})-H(f(T))\qquad\forall\,T>0

(which corresponds to the condition (ii) in Definition
1.8
) and moreover it holds that

(3.36)

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

for all
\Psi_{\delta},\Psi\in L^{2}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
satisfying
\Psi_{\delta}\to\Psi
in
L^{2}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})

as
\delta\to 0^{+}
.

To do this we first take any sequence
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}

satisfying
0<\delta_{n}\to 0\,(n\to\infty)
. From the
L^{2}
-boundedness
(
3.24
) and the criterion
of
L^{2}
-weak compactness, there exists a subsequence

\{\delta_{n_{k}}\}_{k=1}^{\infty}
and a vector-valued
function
{\bf D}\in L^{2}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
such
that

(3.37)

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta_{n_{k}}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot{\bf D}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\quad\forall\,\Psi\in L^{2}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}).\qquad

By convexity of
{\bf x}\mapsto|{\bf x}|^{2}
, this weak convergence together with (
3.23
)
imples that

(3.38)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}|{\bf D}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\leq\liminf_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})-H(f(T))\quad\forall\,T>0.

Next for any
\Psi\in{\cal T}_{c}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
, we have from (
3.37
)
and the result of Step 3 that

(3.39)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot{\bf D}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta_{n_{k}}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t.

Since

\bar{F}_{\delta_{n_{k}}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})1_{\{|{\bf z}|\geq\delta_{n_{k}}\}}+\delta_{n_{k}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2})}

the pointwise convergence

\lim\limits_{k\to\infty}|{\bf z}|\bar{F}_{\delta_{n_{k}}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})
holds
for all
(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\in[0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\setminus\{{\bf 0}\}).

If we denote

\displaystyle g_{k}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}):=\Big|\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}_{\delta_{n_{k}}}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}-\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\Big|,\quad\psi_{*}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|{\bf z}|}}\big|\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\big|.

then, by (
3.8
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}g_{k}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=0\quad\forall\,(t,{\bf w,z})\in[0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\setminus\{{\bf 0}\},

\displaystyle g_{k}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})^{2}\leq f\big(t,\frac{{\bf w+z}}{2}\big)f\big(t,\frac{{\bf w-z}}{2}\big)+|{\bf z}|e^{-\frac{1}{2}(|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2})}=:G(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}).

By definition of
\Psi\in{\cal T}_{c}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
(see
(
6.10
) with
Y=[0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}
)
one sees that
\Psi
has compact support in
[0,T]\times[-R,R]^{3}\times[-R,R]^{3}
for
some
0<T,R<\infty
so that
\psi_{*}^{2}\in L^{1}([0,T]\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}})
.
Also we have
G\in L^{1}([0,T]\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}})
. Then using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the dominated convergence theorem we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Big|\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta_{n_{k}}}}-\sqrt{\bar{F}}\Big|\big|\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\Psi\big|{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}g_{k}\psi_{*}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}g_{k}^{2}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\bigg)^{1/2}\bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\psi_{*}^{2}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\bigg)^{1/2}\to 0\quad{\rm as}\quad k\to\infty.

This together with (
3.39
) leads to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot{\bf D}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}){\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\quad\forall\,\Psi\in{\cal T}_{c}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}).

By Defintion
6.4
,
this means
that the weak projection gradient
\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}

exists and is equal to
{\bf D}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}).

Thus the inequality (
3.38
)
is rewritten

(3.40)

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t\leq\liminf_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})-H(f(T))\quad\forall\,T>0.

Since the weak limit
{\bf D}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}
is unique, it does not depend on the choice of
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
. It follows from
the arbitrariness of
\{\delta_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
(
3.37
) that

(3.41)

\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}}\,{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t

holds for all
\Psi\in L^{2}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}).

Finally for any
\Psi_{\delta},\Psi\in L^{2}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
satisfying

\Psi_{\delta}\to\Psi\,(\delta\to 0^{+})
in
L^{2}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}},{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi_{\delta}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}}\,{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Big|\big(\Psi_{\delta}-\Psi\big)\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}}\,\Big|{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Psi\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle:=I_{\delta}+J_{\delta}\to 0\quad{\rm as}\quad\delta\to 0^{+}

which is because of (
3.41
) and

\displaystyle I_{\delta}\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}|\Psi_{\delta}-\Psi|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\big|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}\,}\big|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}|\Psi_{\delta}-\Psi|^{2}|{\rm d}{\bf w}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}t\right)^{1/2}\to 0\quad(\delta\to 0^{+}).

This ends the proof of the general
weak convergence (
3.36
).

Step 5
. In this step we prove the existence of the weak projection gradient of

\sqrt{ff_{*}/|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}
and the entropy inequalities
(
1.34
) and (
1.29
).
So far we have proved that the weak projection gradient
\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}

exists and belongs to
L^{2}([0,\infty)\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}})
.
Applying Lemma
6.8
to the functions
\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}
and

\sqrt{|{\bf z}|}
, we see that the weak projection gradient

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}
also exists and

(3.42)

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}=\sqrt{|{\bf z}|}\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}.

Also since
\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}=\sqrt{f(t,\frac{{\bf w+z}}{2})f(t,\frac{{\bf w-z}}{2})}
belongs to

L^{2}([0,T]\times{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}})\,\,(\forall\,0<T<\infty)
, we conclude from Lemma
6.9
that the weak projection gradient
\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}(|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}))
also exists
and
3
3

3

Note that here we do not claim the existence of the weak projection gradient

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})
.

(3.43)

\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}(|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z}))=2\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}.

Since

f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})/|{\bf v-v}_{*}|=F(t,{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})
,
it follows from Definition
6.6
that the weak projection gradients

\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\sqrt{ff_{*}/|{\bf v-v}_{*}|},\,\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\sqrt{ff_{*}}
, and
\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\big(ff_{*}\big)
in
{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}\neq{\bf 0}
all exist
and

(3.44)

\displaystyle\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\sqrt{f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})/|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}=2\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\big|_{{\bf w}={\bf v+v}*,{\bf z}={\bf v-v}_{*}},\quad

(3.45)

\displaystyle\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\sqrt{f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})}=2\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\big|_{{\bf w}={\bf v+v}*,{\bf z}={\bf v-v}_{*}},\quad

(3.46)

\displaystyle\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\big(f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})\big)=2\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\big(|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\big)\big|_{{\bf w}={\bf v+v}*,{\bf z}={\bf v-v}_{*}}.\quad

These together with (
3.43
) imply that

(3.47)

\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\big(ff_{*}\big)=2\sqrt{ff_{*}}\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\sqrt{ff_{*}}.

And again by change of variables and using (
3.42
) and (
3.45
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Big|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}=8\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\Big|\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,\Big|_{{\bf w}={\bf v+v}*,{\bf z}={\bf v-v}_{*}}\Big|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle=2\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\big|\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\sqrt{f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})/|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}\big|^{2}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}=D_{L}(f(t)),

Connecting this with (
3.40
) gives

(3.48)

\int_{0}^{T}D_{L}(f(t)){\rm d}t\leq\liminf_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})-H(f(T))\qquad\forall\,T>0.

This implies that
\int_{0}^{\infty}D_{L}(f(t)){\rm d}t<\infty
and
the inequality
(
1.34
) holds true.
Also from
\int_{0}^{\infty}D_{L}(f(t)){\rm d}t<\infty
, (
3.47
), Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and

|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\leq\langle{\bf v}\rangle\langle{\bf v}_{*}\rangle

we have

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\big|\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\big(f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})\big)\big|{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})|{\bf v-v}_{*}|{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}}\sqrt{D_{L}(f(t))}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{1}}\sqrt{D_{L}(f(t))}\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{\|f(t)\|_{L^{1}}\|f(t)\|_{L^{1}_{2}}}\sqrt{D_{L}(f(t))}

so that the integral in the righthand side of
(
1.28
) is absolutely convergent for almost all
t\in(0,\infty)
.

Finally we prove the entropy inequality (
1.29
) under the additional assumption
in the theorem. We will use the following
inequality

(3.49)

|a\log a-b\log b|\leq C_{p}|a-b|^{1/p}+|a-b|\log^{+}(|a-b|)+2\sqrt{a\wedge b}\sqrt{|a-b|}

for
a,b\geq 0
and
1<p<\infty
, where
C_{p}=\frac{p}{e(p-1)}.

[To prove (
3.49
) one may assume that
a>b>0
. Then
|a\log a-b\log b|=|(a-b)\log((a-b)\frac{a}{a-b})+b\log(\frac{a}{b})|\leq(a-b)|\log(a-b)|+(a-b)\log(\frac{a}{a-b})+b\log(\frac{a}{b})
, and so (
3.49
) follows from
the inequalities

|\log x|\leq\frac{q}{e}x^{-1/q}
for
0<x\leq 1
(where

q=p/(p-1)
) and
\log x\leq\sqrt{x-1}
for
x\geq 1
.]
Now assume that

|f_{0}^{\varepsilon}-f_{0}|\big(1+\log^{+}(|f_{0}^{\varepsilon}-f_{0}|)\big)\to 0
in
L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
as

\varepsilon\to 0^{+}
for some
0\leq f_{0}\in L^{1}_{2}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})\cap L\log L({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
.
Using the inequality (
3.49
) with
p=5/4
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle|H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})-H(f_{0})|\leq\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\big|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}({\bf v})\log(f^{\varepsilon}_{0}({\bf v}))-f_{0}({\bf v})\log(f_{0}({\bf v}))\big|{\rm d}{\bf v}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\Big(|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}-f_{0}|\log^{+}(|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}-f_{0}|)+2\sqrt{f_{0}}\sqrt{|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}-f_{0}|}+C|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}-f_{0}|^{4/5}\Big){\rm d}{\bf v}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}-f_{0}|\log^{+}(|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}-f_{0}|){\rm d}{\bf v}+2\left(\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}f_{0}{\rm d}{\bf v}\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}-f_{0}|{\rm d}{\bf v}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+C\left(\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}-f_{0}|(1+|{\bf v}|){\rm d}{\bf v}\right)^{4/5}\left(\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}(1+|{\bf v}|)^{-4}{\rm d}{\bf v}\right)^{1/5}.

Then, by the
L^{1}
-weak convergence
f^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)\rightharpoonup f(t,\cdot)\,({\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0
) for all
t\geq 0
, we have

f(0,\cdot)=f_{0}
, and since

\sup\limits_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}<\infty
, it follows from the above estimate that

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}H(f^{\varepsilon}_{0})=H(f_{0})=H(f(0)).

Thus in this case we see from
(
1.34
) or (
3.48
) that
f(t,\cdot)
satisfies the entropy inequality (
1.29
):

H(f(t))+\int_{0}^{t}D_{L}(f(s)){\rm d}s\leq H(f(0))\qquad\forall\,t>0.

Step 6.
The goal of this step is to prove (
1.27
) so that
f
is an
H
-solution of Eq.(FPL).
Let

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t):=\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)\big({f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}-{f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}\big)\big(\psi+\psi_{*}-\psi^{\prime}-\psi_{*}^{\prime}\big){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}\omega.

Recall the definition of weak solutions for
f^{\varepsilon}
, we have, for any
\psi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(T,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}_{0}({\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}+\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t){\rm d}t\quad\forall\,T\in(0,\infty).

For any
\delta>0
, consider a decomposition

{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t)={\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}_{<\delta}[\psi](t)+{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t)

with

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}_{<\delta}[\psi](t):=\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|<\delta\}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big({f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}-{f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}\big)\big(\psi+\psi_{*}-\psi^{\prime}-\psi_{*}^{\prime}\big){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}\omega,

\displaystyle{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t):=\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon}\big({f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}-{f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}\big)\big(\psi+\psi_{*}-\psi^{\prime}-\psi_{*}^{\prime}\big){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}\omega.

Then using (
3.4
) with
\zeta(r)=1_{\{r<\delta\}},1_{\{r\geq\delta\}}

respectively we have

(3.50)

\displaystyle|{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}_{<\delta}[\psi](t)|\leq C\|D^{2}\psi\|_{*}\sqrt{D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t))}\sqrt{\delta}\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0,

(3.51)

\displaystyle|{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t)|\leq C\|D^{2}\psi\|_{*}\sqrt{D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t))}\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Here the constant
0<C<\infty

depends only on
\sup\limits_{0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}}\|f^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{L^{1}_{2}}.

We claim that for any
t\geq 0
,

(3.52)

{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t)=\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}.

To prove it, we consider a cutoff kernel

\displaystyle B_{0}^{\varepsilon,\eta}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)=1_{\{|\sin(\theta)\cos(\theta)|\geq\eta\}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega),\quad\eta>0

with

\theta=\arccos({\bf n}\cdot\omega)\in[0,\pi]
and
{\bf n}=({\bf v-v}_{*})/|{\bf v-v}_{*}|
.
Correspondingly let
L^{\varepsilon,\eta}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})
and

{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon,\eta}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t)

be defined in (
1.36
) (with
\lambda=0
) and in (
3.52
) respectively
by replacing
B_{0}^{\varepsilon}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)
with

B_{0}^{\varepsilon,\eta}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)
.
Then on the one hand we see from the inequality (
3.4
) with
\zeta(r)=1_{\{r\geq\delta\}}

and the dominated convergence theorem that

\lim\limits_{\eta\to 0^{+}}{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon,\eta}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t)={\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t).

On the other hand, using the first inequality in
Lemma
2.3
and (
1.23
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon,\eta}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega){f^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}\big|\psi+\psi_{*}-\psi^{\prime}-\psi_{*}^{\prime}\big|{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}\omega
