\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\times{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon,\eta}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega){f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}\big|\psi+\psi_{*}-\psi^{\prime}-\psi_{*}^{\prime}\big|{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}\omega

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty}}{4\delta\eta}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\bigg(|{\bf v-v}_{*}|^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}B_{0}^{\varepsilon}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)\sin^{2}(\theta)\cos^{2}(\theta){\rm d}\omega\bigg){f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty}}{\delta\eta}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}{f^{\varepsilon}}{f_{*}^{\varepsilon}}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}<\infty.

From this, we derive that (
3.52
) holds for the collision kernel

B_{0}^{\varepsilon,\eta}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)
, i.e.

(3.53)

{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon,\eta}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t)=\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}L^{\varepsilon,\eta}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}.

By Lemma
2.3
and (
1.23
), we have for all
{\bf v,v}_{*}
satisfying

|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta
that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq\eta<1}|L^{\varepsilon,\eta}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|\leq\frac{1}{\delta}32\|D^{2}\psi\|_{\infty},\quad\lim_{\eta\to 0^{+}}L^{\varepsilon,\eta}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}).

Thus using dominated convergence theorem and letting
\eta\to 0^{+}
, we conclude from (
3.53
)
that

\lim_{\eta\to 0^{+}}{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon,\eta}_{\geq\delta}[\psi](t)=\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(t,{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}.

This proves (
3.52
).

Now let

(3.54)

\displaystyle\psi\in C^{2}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}),\quad\Psi({\bf v,v}_{*})=\nabla\psi({\bf v})-\nabla\psi({\bf v}_{*}),\quad\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})=\Psi\big(\frac{{\bf w+z}}{2},\frac{{\bf w-z}}{2}\big),

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t):=-\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w},

\displaystyle{\cal Q}_{L,\delta}[\psi](t):=-\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}.

Before going further, we need to show that

(3.55)

{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t)=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\psi({\bf v})Q_{L}(f)(t,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v},\quad t\geq 0,

where the righthand side is defined in (
1.28
).
In fact, from the existence of the weak projection gradient
in
{\bf v-v}_{*}\neq{\bf 0}
for the product
f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})
proved in Step 5,
(
3.43
), and (
3.46
) we have (note that all integrals below have been proven to be absolutely convergent for almost all
t>0
)

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}

\displaystyle=8\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\frac{1}{|{\bf z}|}\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot\sqrt{|{\bf z}|\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\sqrt{|{\bf z}|}\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\Big|_{{\bf w}={\bf v+v}_{*},{\bf z}={\bf v-v}_{*}}\,{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle=2\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\frac{\nabla\psi({\bf v})-\nabla\psi({\bf v}_{*})}{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}\cdot\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\big(f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})\big){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}.

Therefore

{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t)=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\frac{\nabla\psi({\bf v})-\nabla\psi({\bf v}_{*})}{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|}\cdot\Pi({\bf v-v}_{*})\nabla_{{\bf v-v}_{*}}\big(f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})\big){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

and so (
3.55
) holds by definition of

\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}\psi({\bf v})Q_{L}(f)(t,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}
in (
1.28
),

Next recalling (
1.30
) for
L[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})
and
using the relation in (
3.54
), we have

\displaystyle L[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\Big|_{{\bf v}=\frac{{\bf w+z}}{2},{\bf v}_{*}=\frac{{\bf w-z}}{2}}=\frac{1}{|{\bf z}|}\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z}),

which implies

\displaystyle\big(f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}+|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\delta e^{-(|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})}\big)L[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\Big|_{{\bf v}=\frac{{\bf w+z}}{2},{\bf v}_{*}=\frac{{\bf w-z}}{2}}

\displaystyle=\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})

and taking integration with change of variable
({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=(\frac{{\bf w+z}}{2},\frac{{\bf w-z}}{2})

(3.56)

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\big(f(t,{\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}_{*})1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}+|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\delta e^{-(|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})}\big)L[\psi]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}){\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\bar{F}_{\delta}\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\bar{\Psi}{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}=-\frac{1}{8}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\bar{\Psi}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\bar{F}_{\delta}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,{\rm d}{\bf z}{\rm d}{\bf w}={\cal Q}_{L,\delta}[\psi](t)

where we have used the equality (
3.31
).
Note that here
\bar{\Psi}
may not
have compact support, but the second equality sign in (
3.56
) is easily proven to hold true by using smooth cutoff approximation
\bar{\Psi}_{n}({\bf w},{\bf z})=\zeta_{n}(|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2})\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})

and the equality

\zeta_{n}(|{\bf w}|^{2}+|{\bf z}|^{2})\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})=\nabla_{{\bf z}}\cdot\Pi({\bf z})\bar{\Psi}_{n}({\bf w},{\bf z})
(see (
6.19
)), where

\zeta_{n}(r)=\zeta(r/n),n\in{{\mathbb{N}}},
and
\zeta\in C^{\infty}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
is the function used in the second part of Step 1.

Now we compute for any
0<T<\infty
and
\delta>0

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t){\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t){\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{<\delta}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t){\rm d}t+\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{\geq\delta}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t){\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}L[\psi]ff_{*}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}L[\psi]ff_{*}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t){\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}|{\cal Q}_{<\delta}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t)|{\rm d}t

\displaystyle+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}L_{0}^{\varepsilon}[\psi]f^{\varepsilon}f^{\varepsilon}_{*}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}L[\psi]ff_{*}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle+\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}1_{\{|{\bf v-v}_{*}|\geq\delta\}}L[\psi]ff_{*}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t){\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle:=I_{\varepsilon,\delta}+J_{\varepsilon,\delta}+K_{\delta}.

By (
3.50
), we first have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}I_{\varepsilon,\delta}\leq C_{0}\|D^{2}\psi\|_{*}\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\int_{0}^{T}\sqrt{D_{0}^{\varepsilon}(f^{\varepsilon}(t))}{\rm d}t\sqrt{\delta}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{T}\sqrt{\delta}.

And using Lemma
2.5
and Lemma
2.6
yield

\lim\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}J_{\varepsilon,\delta}=0
for all
\delta>0.

Finally using (
3.56
) we have

(3.57)

\displaystyle K_{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{L,\delta}[\psi](t){\rm d}t-\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t){\rm d}t\bigg|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\delta\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\times{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}}}|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|e^{-(|{\bf v}|^{2}+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2})}|L[\psi]|ff_{*}{\rm d}{\bf v}{\rm d}{\bf v}_{*}{\rm d}t.

The first term in the righthand of (
3.57
) tends to zero as

\delta\to 0^{+}
by (
3.36
) in Step 4 for the
L^{2}
-integrable functions

\Psi_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=1_{[0,T]}(t)\sqrt{\bar{F}_{\delta}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z}),\Psi(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})=1_{[0,T]}(t)\sqrt{\bar{F}(t,{\bf w},{\bf z})}\,\bar{\Psi}({\bf w},{\bf z})
.
The second term in the righthand of (
3.57
) obviously tends to zero as

\delta\to 0^{+}
by (
2.24
) in Lemma
2.5
.
We thus conclude the convergence

(3.58)

\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t){\rm d}t=\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t){\rm d}t\qquad\forall\,T\in(0,\infty).

From the above convergence we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f(T,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}=\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}(T,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f^{\varepsilon}_{0}({\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}+\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}^{\varepsilon}[\psi](t){\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f_{0}({\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}+\int_{0}^{T}{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t){\rm d}t\qquad\forall\,T\in[0,\infty).

Since
t\mapsto{\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t)
belongs to
L^{1}_{loc}([0,\infty))
, this ensures that

t\mapsto\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}
is absolutely continuous on
[0,\infty)
and (
1.27
) holds true:

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}\psi({\bf v})f(t,{\bf v}){\rm d}{\bf v}={\cal Q}_{L}[\psi](t)\qquad{\rm a.e.}\quad t\in[0,\infty).

This together with the conservation law proved in Step 1
shows that
f
is an
H
-solution of Eq.(FPL) and completes the proof of Theorem
1.12
.
∎

4.
Preliminary lemmas for Isotropic Functions

This section is devoted to some preliminary lemmas for isotropic functions which are crucial
for proving the convergence from Eq.(FD) to Eq.(FPL) and from Eq.(BE) to Eq.(FPL).

4.1.
Useful lemmas

First of all, under the change of variable (
2.1
) and using (
2.4
) (for the
\omega
-representation (
1.2
)), one has a thoroughly detailed representation of
|{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2},|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}
:

(4.1)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle|{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2}=|{\bf v}|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta)+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2}\cos^{2}(\theta)-2\sqrt{|{\bf v}|^{2}|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2}-({\bf v}\cdot{\bf v}_{*})^{2}}\,\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta){\bf k}\cdot\sigma,\\
\\
\displaystyle|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}=|{\bf v}|^{2}\cos^{2}(\theta)+|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta)+2\sqrt{|{\bf v}|^{2}|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2}-({\bf v}\cdot{\bf v}_{*})^{2}}\,\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta){\bf k}\cdot\sigma\end{array}\right.

where

{\bf k}=\frac{{\bf h}-({\bf h}\cdot{\bf n}){\bf n}}{\sqrt{1-({\bf h}\cdot{\bf n})^{2}}}\in{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})\quad{\rm with}\quad{\bf h}=\frac{{\bf v}+{\bf v}_{*}}{|{\bf v}+{\bf v}_{*}|}

and for the case that
{\bf v},{\bf v}_{*}
are linearly dependent,

{\bf k}
is chosen a fixed element in
{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})
independent of

\sigma
.

Lemma 4.1
.

For any
y,z\geq 0
,
\omega,\omega_{*}\in{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}},

let
{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}
,

x=|{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2}/2
and
x_{*}=|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}/2
.
Then for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
and
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
, we have with the
representation (
4.1
)
that

(4.2)

\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}({\bf n})}\big(\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z)\big){\rm d}\sigma\bigg|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big((y-z)^{2}+yz\big(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2}\big)\cos^{2}(\theta)\sin^{2}(\theta),

(4.3)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}({\bf n})}\big(\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z)\big){\rm d}\sigma=-2\pi\big(\varphi^{\prime}(y)-\varphi^{\prime}(z)\big)(y-z)\cos^{2}(\theta)

\displaystyle+2\pi\big(\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\big)yz\big(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2}\big)\cos^{2}(\theta)+R(y,z,\omega,\omega_{*},\theta),

where
R(y,z,\omega,\omega_{*},\theta)
is bounded by

(4.4)

\displaystyle|R(y,z,\omega,\omega_{*},\theta)|\leq C_{0}\Big(\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\cos^{2}(\theta)+\Lambda_{\varphi}\big((\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}||\cos(\theta)|\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\times\big((y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\big)\cos^{2}(\theta),

C_{0}\in(0,\infty)
is an absolute constant, and

\Lambda_{\varphi}(\delta)=\sup_{x,y\geq 0,|x-y|\leq\delta}|\varphi^{\prime\prime}(x)-\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)|,\quad\delta\geq 0.

Proof.

Using
x_{*}-z=-(x-y)
we have

(4.5)

\displaystyle\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z)=\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(z)

\displaystyle=\big(\varphi^{\prime}(y)-\varphi^{\prime}(z)\big)(x-y)+\frac{1}{2}\big(\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\big)(x-y)^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\Big[\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y+t(x-y))-\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z-t(x-y)-\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\Big](x-y)^{2}{\rm d}t.

From (
4.1
) together with the facts
y=|{\bf v}|^{2}/2
and
z=|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2}/2
, we deduce that

x-y=(z-y)\cos^{2}(\theta)-2\sqrt{yz}\sqrt{1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2}}\,\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta){\bf k}\cdot\sigma.

Inserting this into the first and the second terms in the righthand side of (
4.5
),
taking integration and using the fact that

\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}({\bf n})}{\bf k}\cdot\sigma{\rm d}\sigma=0,\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}({\bf n})}({\bf k}\cdot\sigma)^{2}{\rm d}\sigma=\pi,

we obtain (
4.3
) with

\displaystyle R(y,z,\omega,\omega_{*},\theta)=\pi\big(\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\big)(z-y)^{2}\cos^{4}(\theta)

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}({\bf n})}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)\Big[\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y+t(x-y))-\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z-t(x-y)-\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\Big](x-y)^{2}{\rm d}t{\rm d}\sigma.

Since

(x-y)^{2}\leq 4\big((z-y)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\big)\cos^{2}(\theta)
,
|x-y|=\frac{1}{2}\big||{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2}-|{\bf v}|^{2}\big|\leq 2(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\cos(\theta)

and
\Lambda_{\varphi}(2\delta)\leq 2\Lambda_{\varphi}(\delta)
, we obtain
(
4.4
) with
C_{0}=16\pi
.

Next, due to (
4.5
), we have

(4.6)

\bigg|\int_{{\mathbb{S}}^{1}({\bf n})}\big(\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z)\big){\rm d}\sigma\bigg|\leq 52\pi\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\Big((z-y)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\Big)\cos^{2}(\theta).

By replacing
\theta
by
\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta\,(\theta\in[0,\pi/2])
, and using
the equality (
2.5
), we see that (
4.6
) holds if

\cos^{2}(\theta)
is replaced by
\sin^{2}(\theta)
.
Since
\min\{\cos^{2}(\theta),\sin^{2}(\theta)\}\leq 2\cos^{2}(\theta)\sin^{2}(\theta)
, we derive (
4.2
).
∎

Before introducing the next lemma, we state the following facts which are easy to prove. That is,
for any
-1<\alpha<1
and any
y>0,z>0,y\neq z,
we have

(4.7)

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\times{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}}\frac{1}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3+\alpha}}\Big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}=4\pi^{2}\frac{(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})^{1+\alpha}-|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{z}|^{1+\alpha}}{2^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}(1+\alpha)\sqrt{yz}|y-z|^{1+\alpha}},

(4.8)

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\times{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}}\frac{1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2}}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3+\alpha}}\Big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle=16\pi^{2}\bigg(\frac{(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})^{3-\alpha}-|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{z}|^{3-\alpha}}{2^{\frac{3+\alpha}{2}}(1-\alpha^{2})(3-\alpha)y^{3/2}z^{3/2}}-\frac{(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})^{1-\alpha}+|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{z}|^{1-\alpha}}{2^{\frac{3+\alpha}{2}}(1-\alpha^{2})yz}\bigg).

In particular for the case
\alpha=0
, we
have

(4.9)

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\times{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}}\frac{1}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3}}\Big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}=\frac{4\pi^{2}\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{y\vee z}\,|y-z|},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\times{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}}\frac{1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2}}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3}}\Big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}=\frac{8\pi^{2}\sqrt{2}}{3(y\vee z)^{\frac{3}{2}}}.

We are in a position to prove

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
0\leq\alpha<1,y\geq 0,z\geq 0,{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}
. Then

(4.11)

\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3+\alpha}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{1-\alpha}\big(y^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}+z^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}\big),

(4.12)

\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3}(|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{\alpha}\wedge 1)}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{1-\alpha}\big(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}\big),

where
C_{0}
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

In what follows, the notaion
C_{0}
is denoted by any
absolute constant and it may vary in
different lines.

We first prove (
4.11
). By symmetry and approximation (Fatou’s Lemma)
we may assume that
y<z
. Due to the formula (
4.7
), (
4.8
)
and the mean-value theorem, we get

\displaystyle\int_{{{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}\times{{\mathbb{S}}^{2}}}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3+\alpha}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}=(z-y)^{2}4\pi^{2}\frac{(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})^{1+\alpha}-(\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{y})^{1+\alpha}}{2^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}(1+\alpha)\sqrt{yz}(z-y)^{1+\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(z-y)^{2}4\pi^{2}\xi_{1}^{\alpha}2\sqrt{y}}{2^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}\sqrt{yz}(z-y)^{1+\alpha}}\leq\frac{(z-y)^{2}4\pi^{2}2^{\alpha}z^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}2\sqrt{y}}{2^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}\sqrt{yz}(z-y)^{1+\alpha}}\leq 4\pi^{2}2^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}z^{\frac{\alpha}{2}-\frac{1}{2}}(z-y)^{1-\alpha}\leq 4\pi^{2}2^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}z^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}},

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3+\alpha}}{\rm d}\omega\leq 16\pi^{2}yz\frac{(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})^{3-\alpha}-(\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{y})^{3-\alpha}}{2^{\frac{3+\alpha}{2}}(1-\alpha^{2})(3-\alpha)y^{3/2}z^{3/2}}

\displaystyle=16\pi^{2}yz\frac{\xi_{2}^{2-\alpha}2\sqrt{y}}{2^{\frac{3+\alpha}{2}}(1-\alpha^{2})y^{3/2}z^{3/2}}\leq 16\pi^{2}z\frac{2^{2-\alpha}z^{\frac{2-\alpha}{2}}2}{2^{\frac{3+\alpha}{2}}(1-\alpha^{2})z^{3/2}}=\frac{16\pi^{2}2^{\frac{3}{2}(1-\alpha)}}{1-\alpha^{2}}z^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}.

This proves (
4.11
).
Applying (
4.11
) with the cases
\alpha=0
and
0<\alpha<1
respectively,
we have

\displaystyle{\rm l.h.s.\,of\,\,(\ref{4.11})}=\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3+\alpha}}\Big|_{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|\leq 1}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle+\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|^{3}}\Big|_{|{\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}|>1}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{0}}{1-\alpha}\big(y^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}+z^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}\big)+C_{0}\big(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\leq\frac{C_{0}}{1-\alpha}\big(y^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}+z^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}+y^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}\big).

This completes the proof.
∎

4.2.
Crucial lemma for the convergence of isotropic solutions

The following lemma deals with
the convergence of the quadratic terms and zero limit of the cubic terms for isotropic Eq.(FD) and Eq.(BE).

Lemma 4.3
.

Suppose that

B^{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},\omega)
is given by (
(3)
),(
1.11
),(
1.12
)
with
\lambda\in\{-1,+1\}
.
Recall that
L[\varphi](y,z),{\cal J}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](y,z)
and
{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y,z)
are defined in (
1.41
), (
1.47
) and (
1.48
).
Then for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}),F\in{\cal B}_{1}^{+}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})

we have (for any
\lambda\in\{-1,+1\}
)

(4.13)

\sup_{\varepsilon>0}\big|{\cal J}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](y,z)\big|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}(y^{1/2}+y^{1/4}+z^{1/2}+z^{1/4})\qquad\forall\,(y,z)\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2},

(4.14)

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\sup_{(y,z)\in[0,R]^{2}}\big|{\cal J}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](y,z)-L[\varphi](y,z)\big|=0\qquad\forall\,0<R<\infty,

(4.15)

\bigg|\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y,z){\rm d}F(x){\rm d}F(y){\rm d}F(z)\bigg|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon\quad\forall\,\varepsilon>0

where
0<C_{0}<\infty
is an absolute constant. Consequently for any constant
0<M_{0}<\infty
and for the set

{\mathscr{F}}_{0}=\{F\in{\cal B}_{1}^{+}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})\,\,|\,\,\|F\|_{0}\leq M_{0}\}

we have

(4.16)

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\sup_{F\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}}\bigg|\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y,z){\rm d}F(x){\rm d}F(y){\rm d}F(z)\bigg|=0.

Proof.

The proof consists of three steps.

Step 1. Proof of (
4.13
).

Thanks to Lemma 5.2 in
[
13
]
, for any
y>0,z>0
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(4\pi)^{2}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{yz}}\int_{0}^{y+z}{\rm d}x\int_{|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})\wedge(\sqrt{z}+\sqrt{x_{*}})}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Phi_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}\big(\sqrt{2}s,\sqrt{2}Y_{*}\big)\Delta\varphi(x,y,z){\rm d}\theta{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}\sigma_{*}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}|({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*})\cdot\omega|\Phi_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}\big(|{\bf v}^{\prime}-{\bf v}|,|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}-{\bf v}|\big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\varphi(|{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2}/2)+\varphi(|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}/2)-\varphi(|{\bf v}|^{2}/2)-\varphi(|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2}/2)\Big)\Big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\sigma,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\sigma_{*}}{\rm d}\omega.

Then by the definition of

{\cal J}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]
,
for any
y>0,z>0
, we have

(4.17)

\displaystyle{\cal J}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](y,z)=\frac{1}{4\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}\sigma_{*}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}|({\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*})\cdot\omega|\Phi_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}\big(|{\bf v}^{\prime}-{\bf v}|,|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}-{\bf v}|\big)

\displaystyle\times\Delta\varphi\big(|{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2}/2,|{\bf v}|^{2}/2,|{\bf v}_{*}|^{2}/2\big)\big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\sigma,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\sigma_{*}}{\rm d}\omega

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{4\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\sigma,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\sigma_{*}}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}\sigma_{*}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{4\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}{\lambda}E^{\varepsilon}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\sigma,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\sigma_{*}}{\rm d}\sigma{\rm d}\sigma_{*},

where we have used (
2.25
), i.e.,
L^{\varepsilon}_{\lambda}[\varphi]=L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\varphi]+{\lambda}E^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]
.

Let
{\bf z}={\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*}
and write
{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}
,

x=|{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2}/2,x_{*}=|{\bf v}_{*}^{\prime}|^{2}/2
.
From (
2.26
), (
2.27
) and the representation (
4.1
) we have

\displaystyle L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\big(\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z)\big){\rm d}\sigma\bigg){\rm d}\theta,

\displaystyle{\lambda}E^{\varepsilon}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})=\lambda 2\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\bigg(\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\big(\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z)\big){\rm d}\sigma\bigg){\rm d}\theta.

Estimate of
E^{\varepsilon}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]
:  We first prove that

(4.18)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{4\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\big|E^{\varepsilon}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\big|\big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi},2}^{*}(\varepsilon)\big(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{\frac{1}{4}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{4}}\big),\quad y>0,z>0.

For any
y>0,z>0
, thanks to Lemma
4.1
and Cauchy-Schwartz inequality (observing that
\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin^{3}(\theta)=\cos^{5/2}(\theta)\sqrt{\sin(\theta)}\cdot\sin^{5/2}(\theta)\sqrt{\cos(\theta)}
), in the case
{\bf z}\neq{\bf 0}
,
we have

\displaystyle\big|E^{\varepsilon}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin^{3}(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|{\rm d}\theta

\displaystyle\times\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\big((y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{|{\bf z}|t^{5}}{\varepsilon^{4}}\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|t}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|^{2}{\rm d}t\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\big((y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\phi,2}^{*}(\varepsilon)\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf z}|^{3}(|{\bf z}|^{1/2}\wedge 1)},

where we have used Lemma
4.2
with
\alpha=2
.
Thus by (
4.12
), one has

(4.19)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{4\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\big|E^{\varepsilon}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\big|\big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi},2}^{*}(\varepsilon)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz\big(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2}\big)}{|{\bf z}|^{3}(|{\bf z}|^{1/2}\wedge 1)}\Big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi},2}^{*}(\varepsilon)\big(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{\frac{1}{4}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{4}}\big),\quad y>0,z>0.

Estimate of
L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]
:
Using
2|ab|\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
and Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle|L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|\leq 4\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\times\bigg|\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\big(\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z)\big){\rm d}\sigma\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big((y-z)^{2}+yz\big(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2}\big)\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin^{3}(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf z}|^{3}}.

Thus for any
y>0,z>0
, by Lemma
4.2
,
we have

(4.20)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{4\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}|L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})|{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf z}|^{3}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\big(y^{1/2}+z^{1/2}\big).

Putting together the estimates (
4.19
) and (
4.20
), we derive (
4.13
) thanks to (
4.17
). It is easy to see that
(
4.13
) holds also for
y=0
or
z=0
.

Step 2. Proof of (
4.14
).

Applying (
2.26
) to

L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})
and recalling

x:=|{\bf v}^{\prime}|^{2}/2,x_{*}:=|{\bf v}^{\prime}_{*}|^{2}/2
and (
4.1
) we have

\displaystyle{\cal J}_{0}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](y,z):=\frac{2}{4\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}L^{\varepsilon}_{0}[\varphi(|\cdot|^{2}/2)]({\bf v},{\bf v}_{*})\Big|_{{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{4\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}2\Bigg\{\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta

\displaystyle\times\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{1}({\bf n})}\big(\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z)\big){\rm d}\sigma\Bigg\}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta

\displaystyle\times\Big(-2\pi\big(\varphi^{\prime}(y)-\varphi^{\prime}(z)\big)(y-z)+2\pi\big(\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\big)yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\Big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}R(y,z,\omega,\omega_{*},\theta){\rm d}\theta{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle=:I_{1}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)+I_{2}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)

where
R(y,z,\omega,\omega_{*},\theta)
is estimated in (
4.4
).

Estimate of
I_{1}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)
:
Using Lemma
4.2
, (
4.9
), (
4.10
) and recalling that
{\bf z}={\bf v}-{\bf v}_{*},{\bf v}=\sqrt{2y}\omega,\,{\bf v}_{*}=\sqrt{2z}\omega_{*}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)=\frac{1}{\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\Big(\frac{1}{2\pi|{\bf z}|^{3}}-R_{\varepsilon}(|{\bf z}|)\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(-2\pi\big(\varphi^{\prime}(y)-\varphi^{\prime}(z)\big)(y-z)+2\pi\big(\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\big)yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\Big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{1}{|{\bf z}|^{3}}\Big(-\big(\varphi^{\prime}(y)-\varphi^{\prime}(z)\big)(y-z)+\big(\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\big)yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\Big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{2}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}R_{\varepsilon}(|{\bf z}|)\Big(\big(\varphi^{\prime}(y)-\varphi^{\prime}(z)\big)(y-z)-\big(\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\big)yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\Big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle=L[\varphi](y,z)+\sqrt{2}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}R_{\varepsilon}(|{\bf z}|)\Big(\big(\varphi^{\prime}(y)-\varphi^{\prime}(z)\big)(y-z)-\big(\varphi^{\prime\prime}(y)+\varphi^{\prime\prime}(z)\big)yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\Big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}.

This yields that

\displaystyle\big|I_{1}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)-L[\varphi](y,z)\big|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}R_{\varepsilon}(|{\bf z}|)\big((y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{*}(\varepsilon)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf z}|^{3}(|{\bf z}|^{1/2}\wedge 1)}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{*}(\varepsilon)\big(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{\frac{1}{4}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{4}}\big).

Estimate of
I_{2}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)
:  It is not difficult to see that

\displaystyle|I_{2}^{\varepsilon}(y,z)|\leq\frac{1}{\pi\sqrt{2}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}|R(y,z,\omega,\omega_{*},\theta)|{\rm d}\theta{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\times\Big(\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\cos^{2}(\theta)+\Lambda_{\varphi}\big((\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})|{\bf z}||\cos(\theta)|\big)\Big)\big((y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\big){\rm d}\theta{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{5}(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\big((y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos^{3}(\theta)\sin(\theta)}{\varepsilon^{4}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{|{\bf z}|\cos(\theta)}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}\Lambda_{\varphi}\big((\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})|{\bf z}||\cos(\theta)|\big){\rm d}\theta\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\big((y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})\big){\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}A_{\widehat{\phi},2}^{*}(\varepsilon)\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf z}|^{3}(|{\bf z}|^{1/2}\wedge 1)}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}

\displaystyle+C_{0}\int_{{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}\times{{\mathbb{S}}}^{2}}\frac{(y-z)^{2}+yz(1-(\omega\cdot\omega_{*})^{2})}{|{\bf z}|^{3}}{\rm d}\omega{\rm d}\omega_{*}\Omega_{\widehat{\phi},\varphi}(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}A_{\widehat{\phi},2}^{*}(\varepsilon)+\Omega_{\widehat{\phi},\varphi}\big((\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})\varepsilon\big)\Big)\big(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{\frac{1}{4}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{4}}\big),

where

\Omega_{\phi,\varphi}(\delta)=\int_{0}^{\infty}r^{3}\widehat{\phi}(r)^{2}\Lambda_{\varphi}\big(\delta r\big){\rm d}r,\quad\delta\geq 0.

Then for any
y>0,z>0
, we have

\displaystyle\big|{\cal J}_{0}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](y,z)-L[\varphi](y,z)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{*}(\varepsilon)+A_{\widehat{\phi},2}^{*}(\varepsilon)\big)+\Omega_{\widehat{\phi},\varphi}\big((\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})\varepsilon\big)\Big)\big(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{\frac{1}{4}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{4}}\big).

From this together with (
4.17
) and (
4.18
), we derive that

\displaystyle\big|{\cal J}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](y,z)-L[\varphi](y,z)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\Big(\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{*}(\varepsilon)+A_{\widehat{\phi},2}^{*}(\varepsilon)\big)+\Omega_{\widehat{\phi},\varphi}\big((\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{z})\varepsilon\big)\Big)\big(y^{\frac{1}{2}}+y^{\frac{1}{4}}+z^{\frac{1}{2}}+z^{\frac{1}{4}}\big).

This inequality holds also for
y=0
or
z=0
by continuity. From the above inequality and Lemma
4.2
, we complete the proof of
(
4.14
).

Step 3. Proof of (
4.15
).

We will sufficiently use symmetric and cancellation properties
of the cubic terms for isotropic measures.
For notational convenience, we set

\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)=\varphi(x)+\varphi(x_{*})-\varphi(y)-\varphi(z),\quad x_{*}=(y+z-x)_{+}.

It is easily deduced that

(4.21)

|\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)|\leq\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}|x-y||x-z|,\quad x,y,z\geq 0,\,x\leq y+z.

To prove (
4.15
), we note that since the function
(x,y,z)\mapsto{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y,z)=W_{\Phi_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}}(x,y,z)\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)
is continuous and bounded on

{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}
, we can use approximation (see Lemma
5.1
below) to
assume first that the measure
F
is absolutely continuous with respect to the
Lebesgue measure on
{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
,
that is, there is
0\leq g\in L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
such that

{\rm d}F(x)=g(x){\rm d}x.
Then
F\otimes F\otimes F
is also
absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure
on
{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}
. This enables us to avoid discussing tedious behavior of
integration of
{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y,z)
on some
sets of Lebesgue measure zero. So we have

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F=\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F=\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3}}W_{\Phi_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}}(x,y,z)\Delta\varphi(x,y,z){\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{3}4\pi\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x}\frac{\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\bigg\{\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}s}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}+\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}Y_{*}}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}+\lambda 2\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}s}{\varepsilon}\Big)\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}Y_{*}}{\varepsilon}\Big)\bigg\}{\rm d}\theta{\rm d}^{3}F.

Here we have used the fact that
W_{\Phi_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}}(x,y,z)=0
for
y+z\leq x
.
By Lemma 5.3 in
[
13
]
, we have

\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}{\rm d}s\int_{0}^{2\pi}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}Y_{*}}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta=2\pi\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{z}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{y}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{z})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{y})}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}s}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}s.

Thus by exchanging variables
y\leftrightarrow z
and using the symmetry

\Delta\varphi(x,z,y)=\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x}\frac{\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}{\rm d}s\int_{0}^{2\pi}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}Y_{*}}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}\theta{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x}\frac{\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}s}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F.

Then

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F=\frac{16\pi^{2}}{\varepsilon}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x}\frac{\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}s}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle+\lambda\frac{8\pi}{\varepsilon}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x}\frac{\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}{\rm d}s\int_{0}^{2\pi}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}s}{\varepsilon}\Big)\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}Y_{*}}{\varepsilon}\Big){\rm d}\theta{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=:K^{\varepsilon}_{1}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{2}[\varphi].

Estimate of
K^{\varepsilon}_{1}[\varphi]
:  Notice that

\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)|_{x=y}=\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)|_{x=z}=0.

We have the following decomposition for

K^{\varepsilon}_{1}[\varphi]
according to

x<y\wedge z,y\wedge z<x<y\vee z
and
y\vee z<x<y+z
respectively:

\displaystyle K^{\varepsilon}_{1}[\varphi]

\displaystyle=\frac{16\pi^{2}}{\varepsilon}\bigg(\int_{0<x<y\leq z}+\int_{0<x<z<y}+\int_{0<y<x\leq z}+\int_{0<z<x<y}+\int_{0<y\leq z<x<y+z}+\int_{0<z<y<x<y+z}\bigg)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}\,s}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=:K^{\varepsilon}_{1,1}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{1,2}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{1,3}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{1,4}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{1,5}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{1,6}[\varphi].

In the following estimates we will use an equality:

(4.22)

\int_{\sqrt{b}-\sqrt{a}}^{\sqrt{b}+\sqrt{a}}s^{-2}{\rm d}s=\frac{2\sqrt{a}}{b-a}\qquad{\rm for}\quad 0<a<b.

We first compute the sum
K^{\varepsilon}_{1,1}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{1,3}[\varphi]
.
For
K^{\varepsilon}_{1,1}[\varphi]
, it is easy to check that if

0<x<y\leq z
, then
|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|=\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x},(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})=\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x}
.
Thus

\displaystyle K^{\varepsilon}_{1,1}[\varphi]=\frac{16\pi^{2}}{\varepsilon}\int_{0<x<y\leq z}\frac{\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}}^{\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}\,s}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F.

For
K^{\varepsilon}_{1,3}[\varphi]
, if
0<y<x\leq z
, then
|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|=\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y},(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})=\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x}
.
By exchanging
x\leftrightarrow y
, we have

\displaystyle K^{\varepsilon}_{1,3}[\varphi]=\frac{16\pi^{2}}{\varepsilon}\int_{0<x<y\leq z}\frac{\Delta\varphi(y,x,z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}}^{\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x}}\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}\,s}{\varepsilon}\Big)^{2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F.

Let

\Delta_{\rm sym}\varphi(x,y,z):=\Delta\varphi(x,y,z)+\Delta\varphi(y,x,z)=\varphi(z+y-x)+\varphi(z+x-y)-2\varphi(z).

Then

|\Delta_{\rm sym}\varphi(x,y,z)|\leq\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}(y-x)^{2}.

By the second inequality in the assumption (
1.49
) which implies

\widehat{\phi}(\frac{\sqrt{2}\,s}{\varepsilon})^{2}\leq A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2s^{2}}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{1,1}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{1,3}[\varphi]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{16\pi^{2}}{\varepsilon}\int_{0<x<y\leq z}\frac{|\Delta_{\rm sym}\varphi(x,y,z)|}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}}^{\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x}}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2s^{2}}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<x<y\leq z}\frac{(y-x)^{2}}{\sqrt{xyz}}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}}^{\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x}}s^{-2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<x<y\leq z}\frac{y-x}{\sqrt{yz}}{\rm d}^{3}F\leq 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon.

For
K^{\varepsilon}_{1,2}[\varphi]
, if

0<x<z<y
, then
|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|=\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x},\,(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})=\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x}.

Using (
4.21
),

\widehat{\phi}(\frac{\sqrt{2}\,s}{\varepsilon})^{2}\leq A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2s^{2}}

and (
4.22
), we have

\displaystyle\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{1,2}[\varphi]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<x<z<y}\frac{(y-x)(z-x)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}}^{\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x}}s^{-2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<x<z<y}\frac{z-x}{\sqrt{yz}}{\rm d}^{3}F\leq 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon.

For
K^{\varepsilon}_{1,4}[\varphi]
, if

0<z<x<y
, then
|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|=\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z},(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})=\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z}
.
Thanks to facts (
4.21
),

\widehat{\phi}(\frac{\sqrt{2}\,s}{\varepsilon})^{2}\leq A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2s^{2}}
,
(
4.22
) and
x_{*}-z=y-x
, we have

\displaystyle\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{1,4}[\varphi]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<z<x<y}\frac{(y-x)(x-z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}}^{\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z}}s^{-2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<z<x<y}\frac{x-z}{\sqrt{xy}}{\rm d}^{3}F\leq 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon.

For
K^{\varepsilon}_{1,5}[\varphi]
, if

0<y\leq z<x<y+z
, then
|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|=\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{x_{*}},\,(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})=\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z}
.
Due to (
4.21
),

\widehat{\phi}(\frac{\sqrt{2}\,s}{\varepsilon})^{2}\leq A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2s^{2}}
, (
4.22
) and
z-x_{*}=x-y
, we have

\displaystyle\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{1,5}[\varphi]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<y\leq z<x<y+z}\frac{(x-y)(x-z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\frac{1}{2}\int_{\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{x_{*}}}^{\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z}}s^{-2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<y\leq z<x<y+z}\frac{(x-z)\sqrt{x_{*}}}{\sqrt{xyz}}{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<y\leq z<x<y+z}\frac{y}{\sqrt{xz}}{\rm d}^{3}F\quad({\rm because}\,\,x-z<y,\,x_{*}<y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon.

For the last term
K^{\varepsilon}_{1,6}[\varphi]
, if

0<z<y<x<y+z
, then
|\sqrt{y}-\sqrt{x}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|=\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{x_{*}},\,(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})=\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z}
.
Using (
4.21
),

\widehat{\phi}(\frac{\sqrt{2}\,s}{\varepsilon})^{2}\leq A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2s^{2}}
,
(
4.22
) and
z-x_{*}=x-y
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{1,6}[\varphi]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<z<y<x<y+z}\frac{(x-y)(x-z)}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{x_{*}}}^{\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z}}s^{-2}{\rm d}s{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<z<y<x<y+z}\frac{(x-z)\sqrt{x_{*}}}{\sqrt{xyz}}{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{0<z<y<x<y+z}\frac{y}{\sqrt{xy}}{\rm d}^{3}F\quad({\rm because}\,\,x-z<y,\,x_{*}<z)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon.

Patching together all the above estimates, we get

\displaystyle\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{1}[\varphi]\big|\leq\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{1,1}[\varphi]+K^{\varepsilon}_{1,3}[\varphi]\big|+\sum_{2\leq i\leq 6,i\neq 3}\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{1,i}[\varphi]\big|\leq 80\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon.

Estimate of
K^{\varepsilon}_{2}[\varphi]
:  We observe that

\displaystyle Y_{*}=Y_{*}(x,y,z,s,\theta)=\bigg|\sqrt{z-\frac{(x-y+s^{2})^{2}}{4s^{2}}}+e^{{\rm i}\theta}\sqrt{x-\frac{(x-y+s^{2})^{2}}{4s^{2}}}\bigg|\geq|\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{x}|

\displaystyle{\rm for\,\,all}\,\,\,s\in\big[|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|,\,(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})\big]

and

(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})-|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|=2\min\{\sqrt{x},\sqrt{x_{*}},\sqrt{y},\sqrt{z}\}.

Using (
4.21
) and

|\widehat{\phi}(r)|\leq A_{\widehat{\phi}}\frac{1}{r}
for
r>0
, we have

\displaystyle\big|K^{\varepsilon}_{2}[\varphi]\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8\pi}{\varepsilon}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x,|y-x||z-x|>0}\frac{|x-y||x-z|}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}s}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|\Big|\widehat{\phi}\Big(\frac{\sqrt{2}Y_{*}}{\varepsilon}\Big)\Big|{\rm d}\theta{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\pi\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x,|y-x||z-x|>0}\frac{|y-x||z-x|}{\sqrt{xyz}}\int_{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}|\vee|\sqrt{x_{*}}-\sqrt{z}|}^{(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\wedge(\sqrt{x_{*}}+\sqrt{z})}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{sY_{*}}{\rm d}s{\rm d}\theta{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x,|y-x||z-x|>0}\frac{|y-x||z-x|}{\sqrt{xyz}}\frac{\min\{\sqrt{x},\sqrt{x_{*}},\sqrt{y},\sqrt{z}\}}{|\sqrt{x}-\sqrt{y}||\sqrt{z}-\sqrt{x}|}{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\varepsilon\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}^{3},y+z>x,|y-x||z-x|>0}(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})(\sqrt{z}+\sqrt{x})\frac{\min\{\sqrt{x},\sqrt{x_{*}},\sqrt{y},\sqrt{z}\}}{\sqrt{xyz}}{\rm d}^{3}F

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16\pi^{2}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}4\sqrt{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon,

where we have used the inequality

\displaystyle x,y,z>0,y+z>x\Longrightarrow(\sqrt{y}+\sqrt{x})(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{z})\frac{\min\{\sqrt{x},\sqrt{x_{*}},\sqrt{y},\sqrt{z}\}}{\sqrt{xyz}}\leq 4\sqrt{2}.

Collecting the above estimates and setting
C_{0}=(80+64\sqrt{2})\pi^{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F\bigg|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon.

Finally we use approximation to extend the above result to the general measure
F
.
Let
0\leq\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}({{\mathbb{R}}})
be an even function such that

{\rm supp}\zeta\subset[-1,1]
,
\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}}\zeta(x){\rm d}x=1
(i.e.

\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\zeta(x){\rm d}x=\frac{1}{2}
).
Let

g_{n}(x)=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}}n\zeta(n(x-y)){\rm d}F(y)+2F(\{0\})n\zeta(nx),\quad x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},\,n\in{{\mathbb{N}}};\quad{\rm d}F_{n}(x)=g_{n}(x){\rm d}x.

Then
0\leq g_{n}\in L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
and

F_{n}
is absolutely continuous with respect the Lebesgue measure on
{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
.
For any nonnegative Borel measurable function
\varphi
on
{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}
or any

\varphi\in C_{b}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
,
we have, by Fubini theorem, that

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x){\rm d}F_{n}(x)=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{>0}}\bigg(\int_{-ny}^{\infty}\varphi\big(y+\frac{u}{n}\big)\zeta(u){\rm d}u\bigg){\rm d}F(y)+2F(\{0\})\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi\big(\frac{u}{n}\big)\zeta(u){\rm d}u.

Taking
\varphi(x)\equiv 1
and
\varphi(x)\equiv 1+x
, we have

\displaystyle\|F_{n}\|_{0}\leq\|F\|_{0},\quad\|F_{n}\|_{1}\leq 2\|F\|_{1}.

Using dominated convergence theorem we have

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x){\rm d}F_{n}(x)=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(y){\rm d}F(y)\qquad\forall\,\varphi\in C_{b}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}).

For any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
, since the function

{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]
belongs to
C_{b}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3})
, it follows from
Lemma
5.1
with
d=3,s=1
and
\Psi_{n}=\Psi=\varepsilon^{3}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]

that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F_{n}=\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F.

Since we have proved that
\big|\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F_{n}\big|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F_{n}\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon
and

\|F_{n}\|_{0}\leq\|F\|_{0}
for all
n\in{{\mathbb{N}}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\bigg|\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F\bigg|=\lim_{n\to\infty}\bigg|\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F_{n}\bigg|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}\|F\|_{0}^{3}\varepsilon.

This proves (
4.15
) and completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

5.
Proof of Theorem
1.22
and Theorem
1.27

In this section we prove Theorem
1.22
(from Eq.(FD) to Eq.(FPL)) and
Theorem
1.27
(from Eq.(BE) to Eq.(FPL)). We begin by introducing two general known results:

Lemma 5.1
.

(
[
22
]
)
. Given
s\geq 0
. Let

\mu_{n},\mu\in{\cal B}_{s}^{+}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
,
n=1,2,3,...
, satisfy

\sup_{n\geq 1}\|\mu\|_{s}=\sup_{n\geq 1}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}(1+x)^{s}{\rm d}\mu_{n}(x)<\infty,

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\psi(x){\rm d}\mu_{n}(x)=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\psi(x){\rm d}\mu(x)\qquad\forall\,\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}).

Let
d\in{{\mathbb{N}}}
and let
\Psi_{n},\Psi\in C({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{d})

satisfy

\displaystyle\lim_{x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{d}\to\infty}\sup_{n\geq 1}\frac{|\Psi_{n}(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d})|}{\prod_{i=1}^{d}(1+x_{i})^{s}}=0,\quad\lim_{x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{d}\to\infty}\frac{|\Psi(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d})|}{\prod_{i=1}^{d}(1+x_{i})^{s}}=0,

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d})\in[0,R]^{N}}|\Psi_{n}(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d})-\Psi(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d})|=0\qquad\forall\,0<R<\infty.

Then

\lim_{n\to\infty}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{d}}\Psi_{n}(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d}){\rm d}\mu_{n}(x_{1}){\rm d}\mu_{n}(x_{2})\cdots{\rm d}\mu_{n}(x_{d})

=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{d}}\Psi(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d}){\rm d}\mu(x_{1}){\rm d}\mu(x_{2})\cdots{\rm d}\mu(x_{d}).

Corollary 5.2
.

(
[
22
]
)
. Given
s\geq 0
. Let

\{\mu_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal B}_{s}^{+}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
satisfy

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|\mu_{t}\|_{s}<\infty
and, for every
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
,
the function
t\mapsto\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x){\rm d}\mu_{t}(x)
is continuous on
[0,\infty)
.
Let
d\in{{\mathbb{N}}}
and
\Psi\in C([0,\infty)\times{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{d})
satisfy

\displaystyle\lim_{x_{1}+x_{2}+\cdots+x_{d}\to\infty}\sup_{t\geq 0}\frac{|\Psi(t,x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d})|}{\prod_{i=1}^{d}(1+x_{i})^{s}}=0.

Then the function

t\mapsto\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{d}}\Psi(t,x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{d}){\rm d}\mu_{t}(x_{1}){\rm d}\mu_{t}(x_{2})\cdots{\rm d}\mu_{t}(x_{d})\,\,\,is\,\,continuous\,\,on\,\,\,[0,\infty).

The following lemma gives an equivalent definition for measure-valued isotropic weak solution
of Eq.(FPL).

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\{F_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal B}_{1}^{+}({\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0})
satisfy

(5.1)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}\|F_{t}\|_{1}<\infty,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F_{t}=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi]{\rm d}^{2}F_{s}\quad\forall\,\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}),\,\,\,\forall\,t\geq 0.

Then
F_{t}
is measure-valued isotropic weak solution
of Eq.(FPL). In particular if
{\rm d}F_{t}(x)=f(t,x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x
for some

0\leq f\in L^{\infty}([0,\infty),L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},(1+x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x))
, then

f
is an isotropic weak solution
of Eq.(FPL).

Proof.

Recalling (
1.42
) that for any
\varphi\in C^{2}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
we have

(5.3)

|L[\varphi](x,y)|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}(\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y})\qquad\forall\,(x,y)\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}.

We first prove that
F_{t}
conserves the mass and energy.
Following the proof of
Theorem
1.12
, we consider smooth cutoff approximation

\varphi_{R}(x)=(a+bx)\zeta(\frac{x}{R}),\quad x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},\quad R\geq 1.

Here
a,b\in\{0,1\}
,
\zeta\in C_{c}^{\infty}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
satisfies

0\leq\zeta(x)\leq 1
for
x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},

\zeta(x)=1
if
x\in[0,1];\,\zeta(x)=0
if
x\geq 2
.
We have

(5.4)

\varphi_{R}\in C^{\infty}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}),\quad 0\leq\varphi_{R}(x)\leq a+bx,\quad\lim_{R\to\infty}\varphi_{R}(x)=a+bx,\qquad\forall\,x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}

for all
R\geq 1

and

(5.5)

|\varphi_{R}^{\prime\prime}(x)|\leq b|\zeta^{\prime}(\frac{x}{R})|\frac{2}{R}+a|\zeta^{\prime\prime}(\frac{x}{R})|\frac{1}{R^{2}}+b\Big|\frac{x}{R}\zeta^{\prime\prime}(\frac{x}{R})\Big|\frac{1}{R}\leq\frac{C_{\zeta}}{R}\qquad\forall\,x\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},\quad\forall\,R\geq 1.

Here
C_{\zeta}=\sup\limits_{x\geq 0}(2|\zeta^{\prime}(x)|+(1+x)|\zeta^{\prime\prime}(x)|).

Let
C_{*}=\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}\|F_{t}\|_{1}
.
From (
5.2
), (
5.3
) and (
5.1
) we have

(5.6)

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi_{R}{\rm d}F_{t}=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi_{R}{\rm d}F_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi_{R}]{\rm d}^{2}F_{s},\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,\infty),

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi_{R}]{\rm d}^{2}F_{s}\bigg|\leq\frac{C_{0}C_{\zeta}C_{*}^{2}}{R}t\to 0,\quad as\,\,\,R\to\infty\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,\infty).

Thus letting
R\to\infty
we obtain from (
5.4
), (
5.6
) and dominated convergence theorem that

\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}(a+bx){\rm d}F_{t}(x)=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}(a+bx){\rm d}F_{0}(x)\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,\infty).

Since
a,b\in\{0,1\}
are arbitrary, this proves the conservation of mass and energy for
F_{t}
.

Next from the equation (
5.2
) and the bounds (
5.1
), (
5.3
) we see that
for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
,
the function
t\mapsto\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F_{t}
is continuous
on
[0,\infty)
. Then for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
,
using (
5.3
) and Corollary
5.2
with
d=2,s=1
,
\mu_{t}=F_{t}

and
\Psi(t,x,y)=L[\varphi](x,y)
, we conclude that

t\mapsto\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi]{\rm d}^{2}F_{t}
is continuous on
[0,\infty)
, and thus
it follows from (
5.2
) that the function
t\mapsto\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F_{t}

belongs to
C^{1}([0,\infty))
. We therefore conclude from (
5.2
) and
Definition
1.25
that
F_{t}
is a measure-valued isotropic weak solution
of Eq.(FPL).
∎

Now we are in a position to prove the convergence from Eq.(FD) to Eq.(FPL) for isotropic weak solutions.

Proof of Theorem
1.22
(From Eq.(FD) to Eq.(FPL)).

Let
{\cal E}=\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
satisfy
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0\,(n\to\infty)
.
By
isotropic weak form of Eq.(FD) in (
1.46
) with
\lambda=-1
,
we have for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
and
\varepsilon\in{\cal E}
that

(5.7)

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x)f^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x

\displaystyle=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x)f_{0}^{\varepsilon}(x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}{\cal J}_{-1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](y,z)f^{\varepsilon}(y,s)f^{\varepsilon}(z,s)\sqrt{yz}{\rm d}y{\rm d}z

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}\varepsilon^{3}{\cal K}_{-1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y,z)f^{\varepsilon}(s,x)f^{\varepsilon}(s,y)f^{\varepsilon}(s,z)\sqrt{xyz}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y{\rm d}z.

Next, using
the entropy identity (
1.16
)-(
1.17
) with
\lambda=-1
and noting that

0\leq 1-\varepsilon^{3}f^{\varepsilon}\leq 1
and using the same proof
as in the proof of Theorem
1.12
, we have
for the isotropic solutions
(t,{\bf v})\mapsto f^{\varepsilon}(t,|{\bf v}|^{2}/2)
that

(5.8)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},t\geq 0}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}f^{\varepsilon}(t,x)(1+x+|\log f^{\varepsilon}(t,x)|)\sqrt{x}\,{\rm d}x<\infty

and, for any
\varphi\in L^{\infty}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
,

(5.9)

\Omega_{\varphi}(\eta):=\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq\eta}\bigg|\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x)f^{\varepsilon}(x,t_{1})\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x-\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}}\varphi(x)f^{\varepsilon}(x,t_{2})\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x\bigg|\to 0\quad(\eta\to 0)

Applying Lemma
4.3
to the measures
F_{t}^{\varepsilon}
defined by

{\rm d}F_{t}^{\varepsilon}(x)=f^{\varepsilon}(t,x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x
and
M_{0}=\sup\limits_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}f^{\varepsilon}_{0}(x)\sqrt{x}\,{\rm d}x
and recalling that
F_{t}^{\varepsilon}

conserve the mass, we also have for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
that

(5.10)

\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigg|\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{-1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y,z)f^{\varepsilon}(t,x)f^{\varepsilon}(t,y)f^{\varepsilon}(t,z)\sqrt{xyz}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y{\rm d}z\bigg|=0.

From (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and Dunford-Petties criterion of

L^{1}
-weakly relative compactness we conclude that there exist a subsequence of

\{f^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}
, still denote it as
\{f^{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}
, and a
function
0\leq f\in L^{\infty}([0,\infty),L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},(1+x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x))
, such that

f^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)\rightharpoonup f(t,\cdot)\quad({\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0)\quad{\rm weakly\,\,in}\,\,\,L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0},\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x)\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0.

By the way, it is easily deduced from (
5.8
), (
5.9
) and the weak convergence that

(5.11)

\sup_{t\geq 0}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}f(t,x)(1+x+|\log f(t,x)|)\sqrt{x}\,{\rm d}x<\infty,

(5.12)

\sup_{|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq\eta}\bigg|\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x)f(t_{1},x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x-\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{3}}\varphi(x)f(t_{2},x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x\bigg|\leq\Omega_{\varphi}(\eta)\to 0\quad(\eta\to 0)

for all
\varphi\in L^{\infty}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
.

Next using Lemma
4.3
and (
5.3
) we have for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
that

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}|{\cal J}_{-1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y)|,\,|L[\varphi](x,y)|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}(x^{1/2}+y^{1/2}+1),\quad(x,y)\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2},

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{(x,y)\in[0,R]^{2}}|{\cal J}_{-1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y)-L[\varphi](x,y)|=0,\quad\forall\,0<R<\infty.

For any
t\geq 0
fixed, applying Lemma
5.1

with
d=2,s=1
and
{\rm d}\mu_{n}(x)=f^{\varepsilon_{n}}(t,x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x,{\rm d}\mu(x)=f(t,x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x,
and

\Psi_{n}(x,y)={\cal J}_{-1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}[\varphi](x,y),\Psi(x,y)=L[\varphi](x,y)
,
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}{\cal J}_{-1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y)f^{\varepsilon}(t,x)f^{\varepsilon}(t,y)\sqrt{xy}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi](x,y)f(t,x)f(t,y)\sqrt{xy}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y

for all
t\geq 0
,
and so it follows from dominated convergence theorem that

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}{\cal J}_{-1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y)f^{\varepsilon}(s,x)f^{\varepsilon}(s,y)\sqrt{xy}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi](x,y)f(s,x)f(s,y)\sqrt{xy}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Thus taking the limit
\lim\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}
to the equation (
5.7
) we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x)f(t,x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi(x)f_{0}(x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi](x,y)f(s,x)f(s,y)\sqrt{xy}{\rm d}x{\rm d}y

for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
and all
t\geq 0
.
Now applying Lemma
5.3
to the measdure
F_{t}
defined by
{\rm d}F_{t}(x)=f(t,x)\sqrt{x}{\rm d}x
, we conclude that

f
is an isotropic weak solution of Eq.(FPL).
∎

Finally we prove the convergence from Eq.(BE) to Eq.(FPL) for measure-valued isotropic
weak solutions.

Proof of Theorem
1.27
(From Eq.(BE) to Eq.(FPL)).

Let
{\cal E}=\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}\subset(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
satisfy
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0\,(n\to\infty)
. Recall that
for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
,
\varepsilon\in{\cal E}
, we have

(5.13)

\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F^{\varepsilon}_{t}=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}{\cal J}_{{+1}}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{2}F^{\varepsilon}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}\varepsilon^{3}{\cal K}_{{+1}}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F^{\varepsilon}_{s}.

We need to prove that

(5.14)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq\delta}\bigg|\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F^{\varepsilon}_{t_{1}}-\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F^{\varepsilon}_{t_{2}}\bigg|\to 0\quad{\rm as}\,\,\delta\to 0\quad\forall\,\varphi\in C_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}).

In fact from Lemma
4.3
and the conservation of mass and energy we have
for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
that

(5.15)

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},t\geq 0}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}|{\cal J}_{{+1}}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]|{\rm d}^{2}F^{\varepsilon}_{t}\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}(\|F_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{1})^{2}<\infty,

(5.16)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\bigg|\varepsilon^{3}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{3}}{\cal K}_{{+1}}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{3}F^{\varepsilon}_{t}\bigg|\leq C_{0}\|\varphi^{\prime\prime}\|_{\infty}A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}(\|F_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{0})^{3}\varepsilon\to 0\quad({\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0).

These imply

(5.17)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\bigg|\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F^{\varepsilon}_{t_{1}}-\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F^{\varepsilon}_{t_{2}}\bigg|\leq C_{\varphi}|t_{1}-t_{2}|\qquad\forall\,t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0,\,\,\,\forall\,\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}).

where
C_{\varphi}

depends only on
\varphi
,
\sup\limits_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\|F_{0}^{\varepsilon}\|_{1}
, and
A_{\widehat{\phi}}^{2}
.
Now for any
\varphi\in C_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
, using
(
5.17
) and a standard smooth approximation we have

(5.18)

\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq\delta}\bigg|\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F^{\varepsilon}_{t_{1}}-\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F^{\varepsilon}_{t_{2}}\bigg|\leq C_{\varphi}\Lambda_{\varphi}^{*}(\delta)\qquad\forall\,\delta>0

where
\Lambda_{\varphi}^{*}(\delta)=\sup\limits_{|x_{1}-x_{2}|\leq\delta}|\varphi(x_{1})-\varphi(x_{2})|.
This proves
(
5.14
).

As is well-known, the bound
\sup\limits_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E},t\geq 0}\|F^{\varepsilon}_{t}\|_{1}=\sup\limits_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}\|F^{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{1}<\infty
and (
5.14
) imply that
there exists a subsequence of

{\cal E}=\{\varepsilon_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
, still denote it as

{\cal E}
, and a family
\{F_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal B}_{1}^{+}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
, such that

(5.19)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\|F_{t}\|_{1}\leq\|F_{0}\|_{1},

(5.20)

\displaystyle\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F_{t}=\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F^{\varepsilon}_{t}\qquad\forall\,\varphi\in C_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}),\quad\forall\,t\geq 0.

On the other hand from Lemma
4.3
we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in{\cal E}}|{\cal J}_{+1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y)|,\,|L[\varphi](x,y)|\leq C_{\varphi}(x^{1/2}+y^{1/2}),\quad(x,y)\in{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2},

\displaystyle\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\sup_{(x,y)\in[0,R]^{2}}\big|{\cal J}_{+1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi](x,y)-L[\varphi](x,y)\big|=0\qquad\forall\,0<R<\infty.

Thus for any
t\geq 0
fixed, using Lemma
5.1
with
d=2,s=1
and
\mu_{n}=F^{\varepsilon_{n}}_{t}
,

\mu=F_{t}
, and
\Psi_{n}(x,y)={\cal J}_{+1}^{\varepsilon_{n}}[\varphi](x,y),\Psi(x,y)=L[\varphi](x,y)

we obtain

\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}{\cal J}_{+1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{2}F^{\varepsilon}_{t}=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi]{\rm d}^{2}F_{t}

and so the dominated convergence theorem yields that

(5.21)

\lim_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}{\cal J}_{+1}^{\varepsilon}[\varphi]{\rm d}^{2}F^{\varepsilon}_{s}=\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi]{\rm d}^{2}F_{s}\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Taking the limit
\lim\limits_{{\cal E}\ni\varepsilon\to 0}
to the equation (
5.13
)
and using (
5.20
),
(
5.21
) and (
5.16
) we derive for
any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{2}({{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0})
that

\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F_{t}=\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}}\varphi{\rm d}F_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}{\rm d}s\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{2}}L[\varphi]{\rm d}^{2}F_{s}\qquad\forall\,t\geq 0.

Then it follows from (
5.19
) and Lemma
5.3
that

F_{t}
is a measure-valued weak solution of Eq.(FPL).∎

6.
Appendix

In this appendix we prove some general properties that have been used in the previous sections,
and establish the
weak projection gradient.

6.1.
Equivalence of definition for weak formula

We will prove

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
d,m\in{{\mathbb{N}}}
,

X={{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}
or
X={{\mathbb{R}}}_{\geq 0}^{d}
, and let

\{\mu_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a family of positive
measures on
X
satisfying

\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}\mu_{t}(X)<\infty
and that
for any
\psi\in C^{m}_{c}(X)
,

t\mapsto\int_{X}\psi({\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})
is absolutely continuous
on
[0,\infty)
and

(6.1)

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{X}\psi({\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})={\cal Q}_{t}[\psi],\quad{\rm a.e.}\,\,\,t\in[0,\infty)

where
{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi]
has the properties: there exist a function

0\leq M\in L^{1}_{loc}([0,\infty))
and a null set
Z_{0}\subset[0,\infty)
such that
for any
t\in[0,\infty)\setminus Z_{0}
,
\psi\mapsto{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi]
is linear on
C^{m}_{c}(X)
and

(6.2)

|{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi]|\leq\|\psi\|_{m,\infty}M(t)\qquad\forall\,\psi\in C_{c}^{m}(X),\quad\forall\,t\in[0,\infty)\setminus Z_{0}

where
\|\psi\|_{m,\infty}=\sup\limits_{\,{\bf x}\in X}\sum\limits_{|\alpha|\leq m}|{\partial}^{\alpha}\psi({\bf x})|,{\partial}^{\alpha}={\partial}^{\alpha}_{{\bf x}}={\partial}_{x_{1}}^{\alpha_{1}}{\partial}_{x_{2}}^{\alpha_{2}}\cdots{\partial}_{x_{d}}^{\alpha_{d}},|\alpha|=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\cdots+\alpha_{d}.

Then for any
\psi\in C^{m}_{c}([0,\infty)\times X)
,

t\mapsto\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})
is absolutely continuous

[0,\infty)
,
t\mapsto{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(t)]

belongs to
L^{1}_{loc}([0,\infty))
, and

(6.3)

\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}t}\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})=\int_{X}{\partial}_{t}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})+{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(t)],\quad{\rm a.e.}\,\,\,t\in[0,\infty).

Proof.

The proof consists of two steps.

Step 1.
We prove that for any
\psi\in C_{c}([0,\infty)\times X)
,

t\mapsto\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})
is continuous on
[0,\infty)
.

Suppose first that
\psi\in C_{c}(X)
. By extension theorem of continuous functions we
can assume that
\psi\in C_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}^{d}})
.
Let
0\leq J\in C^{\infty}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}^{d}})
satisfy

\int_{{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}}J({\bf x}){\rm d}{\bf x}=1
and

J
is supported on the closed unite ball centered at the origin.
Let
J_{\delta}({\bf x})=\delta^{-d}J(\delta^{-1}{\bf x}),\delta>0
, and

\psi_{\delta}=\psi*J_{\delta}
(convolution).
Then
\psi_{\delta}\in C^{\infty}_{c}({{\mathbb{R}}^{d}})
and

\|\psi_{\delta}-\psi\|_{\infty}\leq\Omega_{\psi}(\delta):=\sup_{{\bf x},{\bf y}\in{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}},|{\bf x}-{\bf y}|\leq\delta}|\psi({\bf x})-\psi({\bf y})|\to 0\quad(\delta\to 0^{+}).

Let
C_{\mu}=\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}\mu_{t}(X)
. For any
t\in[0,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{X}\psi{\rm d}\mu_{s}-\int_{X}\psi{\rm d}\mu_{t}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\mu}\Omega_{\psi}(\delta)+\bigg|\int_{X}\psi_{\delta}{\rm d}\mu_{s}-\int_{X}\psi_{\delta}{\rm d}\mu_{t}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C_{\mu}\Omega_{\psi}(\delta)+\|\psi_{\delta}\|_{m,\infty}\int_{t\wedge s}^{t\vee s}M(\tau){\rm d}\tau.

By first letting
s\to t
and then letting
\delta\to 0^{+}
we conclude that

t\mapsto\int_{X}\psi({\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})
is continuous at
t\in[0,\infty)
.

For general case, let
\psi\in C_{c}([0,\infty)\times X)

and take any
t\in[0,\infty)
. By the uniform continuity of

\psi
on
[0,\infty)\times X
and the above result we see that as
s\to t

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{X}\psi(s,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{s}({\bf x})-\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mu}\|\psi(s)-\psi(t)\|_{\infty}+\bigg|\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{s}({\bf x})-\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})\bigg|\to 0.

So
t\mapsto\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})
is continuous at
t\in[0,\infty)
.

Step 2.
From (
6.1
) we see that for any
\psi\in C^{m}_{c}(X)
,

t\mapsto{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi]
is Lebesgue measurable on
[0,\infty)
. From this and using the following
estimate (
6.4
)
it is not difficult to prove that
for any
\psi\in C^{m}_{c}([0,\infty)\times X)
,

t\mapsto{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(t)]
is also Lebesgue measurable
on
[0,\infty)
.
From the assumption (
6.2
) we have

(6.4)

\big|{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(t)]-{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(s)]\big|\leq\|\psi(t)-\psi(s)\|_{m,\infty}M(t)\leq C_{\psi}|t-s|M(t)

for all
s\in[0,\infty)
and all
t\in[0,\infty)\setminus Z_{0}
.
Here and below
C_{\psi}=\sup\limits_{t\geq 0}\big(\|\psi(t)\|_{m,\infty}+\|D_{1}\psi(t)\|_{m,\infty}\big).

Since

|{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(t)]|\leq C_{\psi}M(t)\qquad\forall\,t\in[0,\infty)\setminus Z_{0}

this implies that
t\mapsto{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(t)]

belongs to
L^{1}_{loc}([0,\infty))
. Also
we have for any
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}<\infty

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{X}\psi(t_{2},{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t_{2}}({\bf x})-\int_{X}\psi(t_{1},{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t_{1}}({\bf x})\bigg|\leq C_{\mu}C_{\psi}|t_{2}-t_{1}|+C_{\psi}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}M(s){\rm d}s.

This implies that

t\mapsto\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})
is absolutely
continuous on
[0,\infty)
.

Since
t\mapsto{\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(t)]

also belongs to
L^{1}_{loc}([0,\infty))
, there is a null set
Z_{1}\subset[0,\infty)

such that

(6.5)

\lim_{t+h\geq 0,h\to 0}\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}{\cal Q}_{\tau}[\psi(\tau)]{\rm d}\tau={\cal Q}_{t}[\psi(t)]\quad\forall\,t\in[0,\infty)\setminus Z_{1}.

Now for any
t\in[0,\infty)\setminus Z_{1}
and
0\neq h\in[-t,\infty)
, look at
the difference quotient

\displaystyle\frac{1}{h}\bigg(\int_{X}\psi(t+h,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t+h}({\bf x})-\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{X}\frac{\psi(t+h,{\bf x})-\psi(t,{\bf x})}{h}{\rm d}\mu_{t+h}({\bf x})+\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}{\cal Q}_{\tau}[\psi(t)]{\rm d}\tau.

Denoting

D_{1}\psi(t,{\bf x})={\partial}_{t}\psi(t,{\bf x})

we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{X}\frac{\psi(t+h,{\bf x})-\psi(t,{\bf x})}{h}{\rm d}\mu_{t+h}({\bf x})-\int_{X}D_{1}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mu}\Lambda_{D_{1}\psi}(|h|)+\bigg|\int_{X}D_{1}\psi(t+h,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t+h}({\bf x})-\int_{X}D_{1}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})\bigg|\to 0

as
h\to 0
, where

\Lambda_{D_{1}\psi}(\delta)=\sup\limits_{t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0,|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq\delta}\|D_{1}\psi(t_{1})-D_{1}\psi(t_{2})\|_{\infty}\to 0
as
\delta\to 0^{+}
.
Also from (
6.4
) we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}{\cal Q}_{\tau}[\psi(t)]{\rm d}\tau-\frac{1}{h}\int_{t}^{t+h}{\cal Q}_{\tau}[\psi(\tau)]{\rm d}\tau\bigg|\leq C_{\psi}\bigg|\int_{t}^{t+h}M(\tau){\rm d}\tau\bigg|\to 0

as
h\to 0
. From these and (
6.5
) we conclude that
the function

t\mapsto\int_{X}\psi(t,{\bf x}){\rm d}\mu_{t}({\bf x})
is differentiable at
t\in[0,\infty)\setminus Z_{1}
and satisfies the equality (
6.3
).
∎

6.2.
Construction of the potential function
\phi

It is given in the following

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\widehat{\phi}(r)
satisfy (
1.11
), (
1.13
) with a constant

0\leq r_{0}<\infty
. Then the limiting function
\phi(\rho)
given by (
1.14
) is well-defined, and
\xi\mapsto\widehat{\phi}(|\xi|)
is the Fourier transform of the function
{\bf x}\mapsto\phi(|{\bf x}|)
in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
where
{\cal S}={\cal S}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
is the class of Schwartz functions on
{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}
. Furthermore if
r_{0}=0
and
\widehat{\phi}(r)\geq 0

in
(0,\infty)
, then
\phi(\rho)\geq 0
in
(0,\infty).

Proof.

To prove the existence of the limit in (
1.14
), we consider

\phi_{R}(\rho)=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}\rho}\int_{0}^{R}r\widehat{\phi}(r)\sin(\rho r){\rm d}r,\quad\rho>0,\,R>0.

Recalling assumptions (
1.13
) and (
1.11
) we know that

r\mapsto r\widehat{\phi}(r)
is monotone in
(r_{0},\infty)
and
r\widehat{\phi}(r)\to 0
as
r\to\infty
.
Therefore using the second mean-value formula of integrals we have for all
R_{2}>R_{1}>r_{0}
that

(6.6)

\bigg|\int_{R_{1}}^{R_{2}}r\widehat{\phi}(r)\sin(\rho r){\rm d}r\bigg|\leq\frac{2}{\rho}\Big(|R_{1}\widehat{\phi}(R_{1})|+|R_{2}\widehat{\phi}(R_{2})|\Big)

which implies that for every
\rho>0
the limit

\phi(\rho):=\lim\limits_{R\to\infty}\phi_{R}(\rho)

exists. Also from (
1.11
) and (
6.6
) we deduce that there is a constants
0<C<\infty
such that

\sup_{R>0}|\phi_{R}(\rho)|\leq C\big(1+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\big)\quad{\rm hence}\quad|\phi(\rho)|\leq C\big(1+\frac{1}{\rho^{2}}\big)\qquad\forall\,\rho>0.

Thus for any
\varphi\in{\cal S}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
, the dominated convergence theorem can be used and we
conclude that
\phi(|\cdot|)\widehat{\varphi}\in L^{1}({{\mathbb{R}}^{3}})
and

(6.7)
